环境和混合样本中流感重配信号推断的计算方法:范围界定审查(预印本)

Computational Approaches for Influenza Reassortment Signal Inference in Environmental and Mixed Samples: Scoping Review (Preprint)
作者： Joseph Sneddon, Manvitha Ganji, Matthew Scotch
摘要中文翻译背景 禽流感 病毒重配是病毒 进化 和致病性、传播性和人畜共患潜力改变的菌株出现的主要驱动因素。环境 监测 已扩展为一种人群水平的流感监测方法,但从环境、混合和混合样本中推断重配信号仍然具有挑战性。目的 本次范围审查旨在检查用于推断和解释环境、混合或混合样本中流感重配信号的计算方法。方法 本次范围界定审查是根据系统评价的首选报告项目和范围界定审查指南的荟萃分析扩展进行的。在 PubMed 和 Scopus 中检索了应用计算方法来推断环境、混合或混合样本中流感重配的研究,并通过引文跟踪确定了其他研究。纳入的研究以样本类型、测序方法和用于重配推断的计算方法为特征。结果 在筛选的 432 份记录中,确定了 48 项研究用于本次审查。大多数研究集中于低复杂性的环境样本,特别是从活禽市场和 野生鸟类 环境中收集的粪便和表面拭子,而高度异质的样本类型(例如城市废水)大多不存在。所有纳入的研究都依赖分段系统发育不一致作为重配推断的主要基础,其中 56.3% 使用序列相似性或遗传距离度量补充树比较。没有研究应用正式的统计系统发育不一致测试或专用的重配特定计算工具。大多数工作流程通过测序前的病毒分离来简化样本的复杂性,从而限制了它们对混合病毒群体的适用性。结论 当前环境流感重配推断的计算方法仍然很大程度上适应于基于分离株的分析,并且不太适合复杂的混合群体样本。未来的 监测 工作将受益于能够解析混合病毒谱系的计算方法,以及标准化的报告实践和测序工作流程,以改善对新兴重配信号的早期检测。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本次范围审查旨在检查用于推断和解释环境、混合或混合样本中流感重配信号的计算方法。
证据基础与方法
本次范围界定审查是根据范围界定审查指南的系统审查和荟萃分析扩展的首选报告项目进行的。在 PubMed 和 Scopus 中检索了应用计算方法来推断环境、混合或混合样本中流感重配的研究,并通过引文跟踪确定了其他研究。纳入的研究以样本类型、测序方法和用于重配推断的计算方法为特征。
共识与结论
当前用于环境流感重配推断的计算方法仍然很大程度上适应于基于分离株的分析,并且不太适合复杂的混合群体样本。大多数研究集中于低复杂性的环境样本,特别是从活禽市场和 野生鸟类 环境中收集的粪便和表面拭子,而高度异质的样本类型(例如城市废水)大多不存在。
争议与缺口
没有研究应用正式的统计系统发育不一致测试或专用的重配特定计算工具。大多数工作流程通过测序前的病毒分离来简化样本的复杂性,从而限制了它们对混合病毒群体的适用性。
科研与实践启示
未来的 监测 工作将受益于能够解析混合病毒谱系的计算方法,以及标准化的报告实践和测序工作流程,以改善对新兴重配信号的早期检测。

Computational Approaches for Influenza Reassortment Signal Inference in Environmental and Mixed Samples: Scoping Review (Preprint)

Authors: Joseph Sneddon, Manvitha Ganji, Matthew Scotch
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND Avian influenza virus reassortment is a major driver of viral evolution and the emergence of strains with altered pathogenicity, transmissibility, and zoonotic potential. Environmental surveillance has expanded as a population-level approach for influenza monitoring, yet inferring reassortment signals from environmental, pooled, and mixed samples remains challenging. OBJECTIVE This scoping review aimed to examine the computational methodologies used to infer and interpret influenza reassortment signals in environmental, pooled, or mixed samples. METHODS This scoping review was conducted according to the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses extension for Scoping Reviews guidelines. PubMed and Scopus were searched for studies applying computational methods to infer influenza reassortment in environmental, pooled, or mixed samples, with additional studies identified through citation tracking. Included studies were characterized by sample type, sequencing approach, and computational methodology used for reassortment inference. RESULTS Of 432 records screened, 48 studies were identified for this review. Most studies focused on low-complexity environmental samples, particularly fecal and surface swabs collected from live poultry markets and wild bird environments, whereas highly heterogeneous sample types such as municipal wastewater were mostly absent. All included studies relied on segment-wise phylogenetic incongruence as the primary basis for reassortment inference, with 56.3% supplementing tree comparisons using sequence similarity or genetic distance metrics. No studies applied formal statistical phylogenetic incongruence tests or dedicated reassortment-specific computational tools. Most workflows simplified sample complexity through virus isolation before sequencing, limiting their applicability to mixed viral populations. CONCLUSIONS Current computational approaches for environmental influenza reassortment inference remain largely adapted from isolate-based analyses and are not well suited for complex mixed-population samples. Future surveillance efforts would benefit from computational methods capable of resolving mixed viral lineages, together with standardized reporting practices and sequencing workflows, to improve early detection of emerging reassortment signals.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This scoping review aimed to examine the computational methodologies used to infer and interpret influenza reassortment signals in environmental, pooled, or mixed samples.
Evidence base and method
This scoping review was conducted according to the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses extension for Scoping Reviews guidelines. PubMed and Scopus were searched for studies applying computational methods to infer influenza reassortment in environmental, pooled, or mixed samples, with additional studies identified through citation tracking. Included studies were characterized by sample type, sequencing approach, and computational methodology used for reassortment inference.
Consensus and conclusions
Current computational approaches for environmental influenza reassortment inference remain largely adapted from isolate-based analyses and are not well suited for complex mixed-population samples. Most studies focused on low-complexity environmental samples, particularly fecal and surface swabs collected from live poultry markets and wild bird environments, whereas highly heterogeneous sample types such as municipal wastewater were mostly absent.
Controversies and gaps
No studies applied formal statistical phylogenetic incongruence tests or dedicated reassortment-specific computational tools. Most workflows simplified sample complexity through virus isolation before sequencing, limiting their applicability to mixed viral populations.
Research and practice implications
Future surveillance efforts would benefit from computational methods capable of resolving mixed viral lineages, together with standardized reporting practices and sequencing workflows, to improve early detection of emerging reassortment signals.

东南亚商业野生动物活动相关的人畜共患疾病风险认知:范围界定审查

Zoonotic disease risk perceptions associated with commercial wildlife activities in Southeast Asia: A scoping review
作者： Thuong Nguyen, Siyeun Kim, Alix Alicia Amelia Day, Jennifer Van Nuil, Mary Chambers, Hongying Li
摘要中文翻译摘要背景:人畜共患疾病的出现与人类与野生动物的相互作用密切相关,特别是在生计、保护和健康交叉的商业野生动物活动中。在东南亚,野生动物狩猎、贸易和农业仍然是许多社区不可或缺的一部分,但关于从事这些活动的社区如何看待相关的人畜共患疾病风险,综合证据有限。本范围审查考察了社区对与商业野生动物活动相关的人畜共患疾病风险的看法,并探讨了它们对 One Health 研究和实践的影响。方法:通过 PubMed 和 Google Scholar 识别 2000 年至 2024 年以英文发表的同行评审研究。审查中纳入了符合条件的研究,该研究考察了社区对东南亚野生动物狩猎、贸易、农业或消费相关的健康或人畜共患疾病风险的看法。数据被提取并按主题进行分析,以确定模式、知识差距以及对研究和实践的影响。结果:来自泰国、印度尼西亚、老挝人民民主共和国和缅甸的八项研究符合纳入标准。研究采用定性或混合方法,涉及猎人、贸易商、农民、消费者和与野生动物接触的社区。研究结果显示,研究人群对人畜共患风险的认识有限,通常仅限于 禽流感 和狂犬病等疾病。由于生计依赖、贫困、获得服务和设施的机会有限以及更广泛的结构性限制,在将认识转化为预防实践方面存在着差距。文化信仰、性别和教育也影响了风险认知和应对措施,非法或非正式贸易环境使参与和数据收集变得复杂。结论:东南亚社区对人畜共患疾病风险的看法是由社会、经济、文化和生计等交叉因素决定的。减少与野生动物商业活动相关的人畜共患疾病风险的有效策略需要采取跨部门、因地制宜的方法,将社区观点纳入保护、公共卫生、农业和兽医部门的沟通和服务中。降低风险战略还应超越提高认识的范围,解决限制社区获取相关信息和服务的结构性障碍,从而支持东南亚更加公平和可持续的人畜共患疾病预防。一份健康影响声明 这项范围界定审查强调了了解从事商业野生动物活动的社区如何看待东南亚人类与动物环境界面上的人畜共患疾病风险的重要性。通过综合健康、社会和生态方面的有限证据,该审查表明,当生计依赖、获得服务的机会有限、社会文化信仰和更广泛的结构性障碍影响决策和行为时,仅对人畜共患疾病风险的认识往往不足以支持预防措施。研究结果强调了通过涉及保护、公共卫生、农业和兽医部门的具体情况敏感的跨部门方法将社区观点纳入同一个健康研究和实施的重要性。虽然社区认知本身并不能构成跨学科的“同一个健康”实践,但了解当地经验、优先事项和限制因素为共同制定更公平、有文化基础和可持续的人畜共患疾病预防策略提供了重要基础。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
禽流感 A 病毒、禽源人畜共患传染病和合格的禽亚型。生物相关的动物、分类学、模型、差异-诊断 和比较实体仅是补充性的,除非还存在材料目标特异性证据;只有显式的词汇或不相关的硬排除才是最终的。
证据基础与方法
通过 PubMed 和 Google Scholar 确定了 2000 年至 2024 年间以英文发表的同行评审研究。审查中纳入了符合条件的研究,该研究考察了社区对东南亚野生动物狩猎、贸易、农业或消费相关的健康或人畜共患疾病风险的看法。数据被提取并按主题进行分析,以确定模式、知识差距以及对研究和实践的影响。
共识与结论
东南亚社区对人畜共患疾病风险的看法是由社会、经济、文化和生计等交叉因素决定的。当生计依赖、获得服务的机会有限、社会文化信仰和更广泛的结构性障碍影响决策和行为时,仅对人畜共患疾病风险的认识往往不足以支持预防措施。
争议与缺口
由于生计依赖、贫困、获得服务和设施的机会有限以及更广泛的结构性限制,在将认识转化为预防实践方面存在着差距。文化信仰、性别和教育也影响了风险认知和应对措施,非法或非正式贸易环境使参与和数据收集变得复杂。
科研与实践启示
减少与野生动物商业活动相关的人畜共患疾病风险的有效策略需要采取跨部门、因地制宜的方法,将社区观点纳入保护、公共卫生、农业和兽医部门的沟通和服务中。降低风险战略还应超越提高认识的范围,解决限制社区获取相关信息和服务的结构性障碍,从而支持东南亚更加公平和可持续的人畜共患疾病预防。

Zoonotic disease risk perceptions associated with commercial wildlife activities in Southeast Asia: A scoping review

Authors: Thuong Nguyen, Siyeun Kim, Alix Alicia Amelia Day, Jennifer Van Nuil, Mary Chambers, Hongying Li
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background : Zoonotic disease emergence is closely linked to human-wildlife interactions, particularly in commercial wildlife activities where livelihoods, conservation, and health intersect. In Southeast Asia, wildlife hunting, trade, and farming remain integral to many communities, yet limited evidence has been synthesized on how communities engaged in these activities perceive associated zoonotic disease risks. This scoping review examines community perceptions of zoonotic disease risks associated with commercial wildlife activities and explores their implications for One Health research and practice. Methods : Peer-reviewed studies published in English between 2000 and 2024 were identified through PubMed and Google Scholar. Eligible studies examining community perceptions of health or zoonotic disease risks associated with wildlife hunting, trade, farming, or consumption in Southeast Asia were included in the review. Data were extracted and analyzed thematically to identify patterns, knowledge gaps, and implications for research and practice. Results : Eight studies from Thailand, Indonesia, the Lao People’s Democratic Republic, and Myanmar met the inclusion criteria. Studies used qualitative or mixed-methods approaches involving hunters, traders, farmers, consumers, and communities with wildlife contact. Study findings showed partial awareness of zoonotic risks among study populations, often limited to diseases such as avian influenza and rabies. Gaps in translating awareness into preventive practices were highlighted, driven by livelihood dependence, poverty, limited access to services and facilities, and broader structural constraints. Cultural beliefs, gender, and education also shaped risk perceptions and responses, and illegal or informal trade contexts complicated engagement and data collection. Conclusions : Community perceptions of zoonotic disease risk in Southeast Asia are shaped by intersecting social, economic, cultural, and livelihood factors. Effective strategies to reduce zoonotic disease risk associated with commercial wildlife activities require cross-sectoral, context-sensitive approaches that integrate community perspectives into communication and services across the conservation, public health, agriculture, and veterinary sectors. Risk reduction strategies should also move beyond awareness raising to address structural barriers that limit communities’ access to relevant information and services, thereby supporting more equitable and sustainable zoonotic disease prevention in Southeast Asia. One Health impact statement This scoping review highlights the importance of understanding how communities engaged in commercial wildlife activities perceive zoonotic disease risks at the human-animal-environment interfaces in Southeast Asia. By synthesizing limited evidence across health, social, and ecological dimensions, the review demonstrates that awareness of zoonotic disease risks alone is often insufficient to support preventive practices when livelihood dependence, limited access to services, sociocultural beliefs, and broader structural barriers shape decision-making and behaviors. The findings underscore the importance of integrating community perspectives into One Health research and implementation through context-sensitive, cross-sectoral approaches involving conservation, public health, agriculture, and veterinary sectors. While community perceptions alone do not constitute transdisciplinary One Health practice, understanding local experiences, priorities, and constraints provides a critical foundation for coproducing more equitable, culturally grounded, and sustainable zoonotic disease prevention strategies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Avian influenza A viruses, avian-origin zoonotic infections, and qualified avian subtypes. Biologically related animal, taxonomic, model, differential-diagnosis and comparative entities are supplementary-only unless material target-specific evidence is also present; only explicit lexical or unrelated hard exclusions are terminal.
Evidence base and method
Peer-reviewed studies published in English between 2000 and 2024 were identified through PubMed and Google Scholar. Eligible studies examining community perceptions of health or zoonotic disease risks associated with wildlife hunting, trade, farming, or consumption in Southeast Asia were included in the review. Data were extracted and analyzed thematically to identify patterns, knowledge gaps, and implications for research and practice.
Consensus and conclusions
Community perceptions of zoonotic disease risk in Southeast Asia are shaped by intersecting social, economic, cultural, and livelihood factors. Awareness of zoonotic disease risks alone is often insufficient to support preventive practices when livelihood dependence, limited access to services, sociocultural beliefs, and broader structural barriers shape decision-making and behaviors.
Controversies and gaps
Gaps in translating awareness into preventive practices were highlighted, driven by livelihood dependence, poverty, limited access to services and facilities, and broader structural constraints. Cultural beliefs, gender, and education also shaped risk perceptions and responses, and illegal or informal trade contexts complicated engagement and data collection.
Research and practice implications
Effective strategies to reduce zoonotic disease risk associated with commercial wildlife activities require cross-sectoral, context-sensitive approaches that integrate community perspectives into communication and services across the conservation, public health, agriculture, and veterinary sectors. Risk reduction strategies should also move beyond awareness raising to address structural barriers that limit communities’ access to relevant information and services, thereby supporting more equitable and sustainable zoonotic disease prevention in Southeast Asia.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

亚太区域流感网络加强对 禽流感 防范工作的关注 - 世界卫生组织 (WHO)

Asia-Pacific regional influenza network sharpens focus on avian influenza preparedness - World Health Organization (WHO)
新闻简报世界卫生组织 (WHO) 西太平洋区域办事处报告了 2026 年 8 月 25 日至 27 日举行的第 19 届国家流感中心和流感 监测 双区域会议。会议重点讨论了 禽流感 的防备情况,特别是针对整个地区鸟类中 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) A(H5N1) 病毒 暴发 的增加亚太地区。强调了 跨物种溢出 对人类的潜力,强调需要快速检测和响应。来自世界卫生组织西太平洋和东南亚地区的 150 多名流感专家、实验室科学家和公共卫生官员参加了此次活动。会议旨在加强监测系统,加强数据共享,提高基因组测序能力,以更好地管理威胁。世界卫生组织敦促与会者实施讨论的战略,并继续合作努力解决 禽流感 带来的人畜共患病风险。响应状态正在调查中,重点是准备和协调。
查看新闻五要素
时间
第19次国家流感中心和流感监测双区域会议于2026年8月25日至27日举行。新闻报道的发布日期为2026年8月27日。
地点与对象
会议涉及世卫组织西太平洋区域的国家和地区,包括澳大利亚、新西兰和太平洋其他国家和地区。重点是该地区的 禽流感 暴发,特别是鸟类,对人类也有潜在的 跨物种溢出。
事件
亚太区域流感网络通过第 19 届国家流感中心和流感 监测 双区域会议强调了针对 禽流感,特别是 HPAI A(H5N1) 的准备。世界卫生组织(WHO)报道了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
会议讨论了鸟类中 HPAI A(H5N1) 不断增加的 暴发,这对人类构成了潜在的 跨物种溢出 风险。证据中没有量化影响的规模,但该风险被认为是一种人畜共患威胁,需要迅速检测和应对。
应对、状态与不确定性
会议旨在加强 监测、数据共享和基因组测序能力,以应对 HPAI A(H5N1) 的传播。响应状态正在调查中,重点是准备和协调。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Asia-Pacific regional influenza network sharpens focus on avian influenza preparedness - World Health Organization (WHO)

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) Regional Office for the Western Pacific reported on the 19th Biregional Meeting of National Influenza Centres and Influenza Surveillance, held from 25 to 27 August 2026. The meeting focused on avian influenza preparedness, particularly in response to the increasing outbreaks of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) A(H5N1) in birds across the Asia-Pacific region. The potential for spillover to humans was highlighted, emphasizing the need for swift detection and response. The event involved over 150 influenza experts, laboratory scientists, and public health officials from the WHO Western Pacific and Southeast Asia regions. The meeting aimed to strengthen surveillance systems, enhance data sharing, and improve genomic sequencing capacity to better manage the threat. The WHO urged participants to implement the strategies discussed and continue collaborative efforts to address the zoonotic risk posed by avian influenza. The response status was under investigation, with a focus on preparedness and coordination.

Donors Regional health initiatives Contact us Careers RCM 2026 Home/ News/ Asia-Pacific regional influenza network sharpens focus on avian influenza preparedness © WHO / Puthealin Sok A laboratory technician works on virus genome sequencing analysis in Cambodia. © Credits Asia-Pacific regional influenza network sharpens focus on avian influenza preparedness 19th National Influenza Centres Meeting urges region to strengthen efforts as avian influenza A(H5N1) outbreaks increase 27 August 2026 Media release Reading time: 4 min (993 words) As more countries in the Asia-Pacific region grapple with outbreaks of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) A(H5N1) impacting animals, with the potential for spillover to humans, countries are being urged to ensure their health systems are prepared to swiftly detect and respond to this zoonotic threat. This week, over 150 influenza experts, laboratory scientists and public health officials concluded the 19th Biregional Meeting of National Influenza Centres and Influenza Surveillance co-organized by the World Health Organization (WHO) Regional Office for South-East Asia and Regional Office for the Western Pacific. The three-day online gathering from 25 to 27 August 2026 examined how surveillance data can be turned into faster, more effective public health decisions, including in response to the ongoing spread of HPAI A(H5N1) in birds in the Pacific. The meeting brought together influenza programmes, national influenza laboratories, WHO collaborating centres and H5 reference laboratories to craft a common approach to influenza and respiratory virus surveillance, in line with the Global Influenza Strategy (2019–2030) and the Pandemic Influenza Preparedness (PIP) Framework. Spotlight on A(H5N1) in the Pacific A central highlight of this year’s meeting was a dedicated session on One Health coordination mechanisms, featuring case studies on the region’s response to the incursions of HPAI A(H5N1) in Australia and New Zealand and its threat to the wider Pacific region. One Health underscores the interconnectedness of animal, human and environmental health, bringing multiple sectors together to strengthen health emergency preparedness and response, including pandemic preparedness. Australia presented on its national responses following the recent incursion of the virus in bird populations, as well as preparedness measures taken in the Pacific in anticipation of further spread. Bangladesh offered a country perspective on how One Health coordination connects human, animal and laboratory surveillance to support early detection, information-sharing and joint investigation of zoonotic influenza signals. In response to the threat posed by HPAI A(H5N1), WHO convened regional partners and multisectoral experts including the Food and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations (FAO), the United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP), the World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH), the Pacific Community (SPC) and national public health, animal and wildlife experts to develop a Rapid Joint One Health Risk Assessment (JRA). The JRA determines the risk of incursion of HPAI A(H5N1) into Pacific island countries and areas to identify key preparedness activities and ensure national readiness to respond. “Although influenza surveillance is one of the world’s oldest and most mature disease surveillance systems, the recent HPAI A(H5N1) arrival in Australia and New Zealand is a sharp reminder that vigilance cannot lapse,” said Dr Saia Ma’u Piukala, WHO Regional Director for the Western Pacific. “Effective One Health coordination - linking human, animal and environmental health authorities - is essential to detecting, characterizing and acting on zoonotic threats quickly enough to protect both public health and livelihoods.” From data to decisions Beyond the A(H5N1) case studies, participants reviewed the state of seasonal and zoonotic influenza across the two regions, with country presentations ranging from Cambodia’s reflections on two decades of surveillance experience to Papua New Guinea’s rollout of an integrated respiratory disease surveillance system. Nepal highlighted how the application of the Zoonotic Influenza Distribution Assessment and Ranking (ZIDAR) system can support the development of comprehensive One Health surveillance and help countries prioritize targeted interventions. India demonstrated how integrating influenza surveillance into the Integrated Health Information Platform (IHIP) platform provides near-real-time data for rapid analysis and visualization of influenza trends, supporting timely outbreak detection and response. Sessions also introduced practical tools to help countries convert surveillance data into action, including updates on the Pandemic Influenza Severity Assessment (PISA) framework, burden-of-disease studies, and WHO’s Public Health and Social Measures (PHSM) Decision Navigator tool, which will soon include an influenza-specific module. A network built on solidarity The meeting marked a milestone for the Global Influenza Surveillance and Response System (GISRS), which approaches its 75th anniversary in 2027. Participants welcomed a new WHO Collaborating Centre for Quality and Capacity Development with GISRS - the Public Health Laboratory Services Branch of the Centre for Health Protection, Department of Health, in Hong Kong SAR (China). They also congratulated Brunei Darussalam on the designation of its new National Influenza Centre, demonstrating that the network is continuing to grow and strengthen. “Our strength is in our network - in sharing expertise, information and capacities, and in supporting one another to be better prepared for the threats ahead,” said Dr Nilesh Buddha, Officer in Charge for the WHO Regional Office for South-East Asia. “Where expertise exists in one country, we should facilitate its transfer and application across the region; where specialized capacities are available, we should strengthen pathways for countries to access them.” Dr Buddha also stressed the importance of coordinated investments, resource mobilization and strategic prioritization at a time when resources are increasingly constrained. He called for greater collaboration to make optimal use of existing capacities, maximize impact and avoid duplication. In her closing remarks at the meeting, Dr Gina Samaan, the WHO Regional Emergency Director for the Western Pacific, called on participants to carry the ideas, contacts and tools discussed over the three days back to their national teams, and to continue working in solidarity on the timely sharing of influenza viruses and data, accurate virus characterization, and strengthened genomic sequencing capacity. She assured participants that WHO would continue to offer technical guidance and capacity-building support to Member States. “Surveillance data only matter when they change what public health systems do,” she said. “Information that sits in a spreadsheet protect no one, while information that reaches a decision-maker at the right moment saves lives.” The 19th Biregional Meeting of National Influenza Centres and Influenza Surveillance reaffirmed that as influenza A(H5N1) continues to test animal and human health systems, the strength of this surveillance network - and the countries and institutions that sustain it - remains one of the region’s most important lines of defence. ____________________ For more information, please contact: WHO South-East Asia Communications, SEMediaRelations@who.int WHO Western Pacific Communications, wprocom@who.int Fact sheets Zoonoses 29 July 2020 One Health 8 May 2026 Influenza (avian and other zoonotic) 21 January 2026 More Related links: Global Influenza Strategy 2019–2030 Pandemic Influenza Preparedness (PIP) Framework

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

美洲地区流行病学更新 禽流感 A(H5) - 2026 年 8 月 26 日 - ReliefWeb

Epidemiological Update Avian Influenza A(H5) in the Americas Region - 26 August 2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报该报告由 ReliefWeb 于 2026 年 8 月 26 日发布,提供了美洲地区 禽流感 A(H5) 病毒的最新流行病学信息。报告强调,自1996年以来,A/鹅/广东/1/96谱系在鸟类种群中引起了暴发的反复出现,并导致数百人因接触受感染的动物或环境而感染病例和死亡。当前更新指出,A(H5N1) 和其他 禽流感 A(H5) 病毒已在全球多个地理区域的鸟类和哺乳动物中发现。2021 年至 2026 年 3 月期间,据报告有 600 多种鸟类和 100 多种哺乳动物受到具有人畜共患潜力的 禽流感 病毒(例如 A(H5Nx))的影响。与 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) A(H5) 病毒相关的全球公共卫生风险被评估为低,职业或经常接触的人的风险估计为低至中度。该报告是泛美卫生组织的最新情况,表明正在对局势进行监测和评估,但没有提及积极的遏制措施或进一步调查。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于2026年8月26日发布。更新涵盖2021年至2026年3月的数据,并于2026年6月17日进行了额外更新。
地点与对象
该报告重点关注美洲地区,提到了阿根廷和其他未具体说明的国家。它包括野生鸟类和家禽鸟类,以及遍布全球多个地理区域的哺乳动物。
事件
泛美卫生组织报告了 禽流感 A(H5) 病毒(包括 A(H5N1) 亚型和其他 禽流感 A(H5) 病毒)的流行病学最新情况。该更新详细介绍了这些病毒在鸟类和哺乳动物中的传播情况,并指出全球公共卫生风险较低。
规模、影响与风险
2021 年至 2026 年 3 月期间,据报告有 600 多种野生鸟类和家禽以及 100 多种哺乳动物受到具有人畜共患潜力的 禽流感 病毒(例如 A(H5Nx))的影响。据估计,与 HPAI A(H5) 病毒相关的全球公共卫生风险较低,对于职业或经常接触的人来说,风险为低至中度。
应对、状态与不确定性
该报告是泛美卫生组织的最新流行病学报告,表明对情况的持续监测和评估。对于特定群体,风险级别被明确规定为低和低至中度,但没有提及积极的调查或遏制措施。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Epidemiological Update Avian Influenza A(H5) in the Americas Region - 26 August 2026 - ReliefWeb

News Brief
This report, published by ReliefWeb on 26 August 2026, provides an epidemiological update on avian influenza A(H5) viruses in the Americas region. It highlights that since 1996, the A/goose/Guangdong/1/96 lineage has caused recurrent outbreaks in bird populations and resulted in hundreds of human cases and deaths due to exposure to infected animals or environments. The current update notes that A(H5N1) and other avian influenza A(H5) viruses have been identified in birds and mammals across multiple geographic regions worldwide. Between 2021 and March 2026, over 600 bird species and more than 100 mammal species have been reported as affected by avian influenza viruses with zoonotic potential, such as A(H5Nx). The global public health risk associated with highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) A(H5) viruses is assessed as low, with the risk for people with occupational or frequent exposure estimated to be low to moderate. The report is an update from PAHO, indicating ongoing monitoring and assessment of the situation, with no mention of active containment measures or further investigation.
Original source text (English)

Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA Argentina + 9 more Epidemiological Update Avian Influenza A(H5) in the Americas Region - 26 August 2026 Format Situation Report Source PAHO Posted 26 Aug 2026 Originally published 26 Aug 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 829.05 KB) Avian influenza A(H5) is a highly contagious viral disease that primarily affects poultry and wild birds, with the ability to sporadically infect mammalian species, including humans. Since 1996, the A/goose/Guangdong/1/96 lineage has caused recurrent outbreaks in bird populations, as well as hundreds of human cases and deaths resulting from exposure to infected animals or contaminated environments. Currently, the A(H5N1) subtype and other avian influenza A(H5) viruses have been identified in birds and mammals across multiple geographic regions worldwide. Between 2021 and March 2026, more than 600 species of wild and poultry birds and more than 100 species of mammals have been reported as affected by avian influenza viruses with zoonotic potential, such as A(H5Nx). In May 2026, the Food and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations (FAO), the World Organization for Animal Health (WOAH), and the World Health Organization (WHO) determined that the global public health risk associated with highly pathogenic avian influenza viruses (HPAI) A(H5) belonging to the Gs/Gd lineage remains low. Similarly, for people with occupational or frequent exposure, the risk was estimated to be low to moderate. Scale, impact and risk
Between 2021 and March 2026, more than 600 species of wild and poultry birds and more than 100 species of mammals have been reported as affected by avian influenza viruses with zoonotic potential, such as A(H5Nx). A high priorityPublic-health news

Avian influenza in the Americas: animal outbreaks decline, but PAHO calls for continued vigilance - Pan American Health Organization (PAHO)

News Brief
A Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) webpage dated 27 August 2026 lists a news item titled 'Avian influenza in the Americas: animal outbreaks decline, but PAHO calls for continued vigilance.' The listing also points to a related Epidemiological Update, 'Avian Influenza A(H5) in the Americas Region,' dated 26 August 2026. The headline suggests a regional decline in animal outbreaks of avian influenza, particularly subtype A(H5), while urging ongoing vigilance. However, the supplied evidence consists only of the PAHO website menu and news/alert listing text; the body of the news article and the full Epidemiological Update were not included. As a result, no specific countries, animal species, human cases, case or death counts, transmission details, or concrete response measures (such as surveillance, testing, culling, or vaccination activities) can be confirmed from the supplied evidence. The status of the situation therefore remains as indicated by the headline and update title, with the principal source-body limitation being that the detailed report content was not available for review.

News Brief
Australia has confirmed its first case of H5N1 bird flu in a seal, marking a significant development in the ongoing outbreak. The virus was detected in a dead long-nosed fur seal in South Australia, according to authorities. This report highlights the potential spread of the H5N1 strain beyond avian hosts, raising concerns about its impact on marine mammals. However, there is no mention of human cases, deaths, or exposures related to this event. The report does not specify any investigation or response measures taken by health officials, leaving the broader implications of this finding unclear. The evidence is based on a news report from AnewZ, which provides the confirmation of the seal case but lacks detailed information on the outbreak's scale or risk assessment.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳大利亚确诊海豹H5N1禽流感病例 - 台北时报

Australia confirms H5N1 bird flu case in fur seal - Taipei Times
新闻简报澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感病例,这只长鼻海狗在南澳大利亚海滩港被发现死亡。这种病毒一直在自然环境中传播,首先在迁徙海鸟中发现,然后才传播到本地物种。南澳大利亚当局报告了这起案件,并指出海豹的死亡似乎与其他患病的海洋哺乳动物无关。澳大利亚农业、渔业和林业部长朱莉·柯林斯强调,一旦病毒在环境中传播,就很难避免重大损失。澳大利亚的家禽或农业中尚未发现H5N1病毒,全国已有297例确诊病例。海豹因其沿海栖息地且靠近 野生鸟类 而面临高风险。自 6 月开始的 暴发 已导致本月海鸟大规模死亡,突显该病毒对野生动物和生态系统的威胁日益严重。
查看新闻五要素
时间
Mon, Aug 24, 2026 (event date), 2026-08-23 (publication date).
地点与对象
South Australia, coastal town of Beachport, approximately 310 km south of Adelaide, with a fur seal population estimated at 100,000 in the state’s waters.
事件
澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感病例,特别是在南澳大利亚海滩港发现死亡的长鼻海狗。南澳大利亚当局和澳大利亚农业、渔业和林业部长报告了此案。
规模、影响与风险
这标志着澳大利亚首次在哺乳动物中检测到 H5N1 病毒。该病毒已在自然环境中传播,造成重大损失。H5N1尚未出现在澳大利亚的家禽或农业中,全国已有297例确诊病例。由于靠近 野生鸟类,海豹面临高风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
此案已得到南澳大利亚当局证实。部长表示,病毒正在自然环境中传播,海豹的死亡与其他患病或死亡的海洋哺乳动物无关。暴发 是在迁徙海鸟中检测到病毒并传播到本地物种后开始的。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Australia confirms H5N1 bird flu case in fur seal - Taipei Times

News Brief
Australia has confirmed its first case of H5N1 bird flu in a mammal, a long-nosed fur seal found dead in Beachport, South Australia. The virus, which has been spreading in the natural environment, was first detected in migratory seabirds before moving to native species. South Australian authorities reported the case, noting that the seal’s death does not appear linked to other sick marine mammals. The Australian Minister for Agriculture, Fisheries and Forestry, Julie Collins, emphasized the difficulty of avoiding significant losses once the virus is established in the environment. H5N1 has not been found in Australia’s poultry or agriculture, with 297 confirmed cases nationwide. Seals are at high risk due to their coastal habitat and proximity to wild birds. The outbreak, which began in June, has led to mass seabird deaths this month, highlighting the growing threat of the virus to wildlife and ecosystems.
Original source text (English)
Front Page Taiwan News Business Editorial & Opinion Sports World News Features Bilingual Pages Search Search All Front Page Taiwan News Business Editorial & Opinion Sports World News Features Bilingual Pages July 30, 2026 - August 28, 2026 GO Home World News Mon, Aug 24, 2026 page5 Australia confirms H5N1 bird flu case in fur seal Reuters, SYDNEY Add TT as Preferred Source Australia yesterday confirmed its first case of H5N1 bird flu in a mammal, with the deadly virus found in a long-nosed fur seal in South Australia. Australia has been grappling with rising cases of the highly pathogenic avian influenza after it landed in the country in June. The country’s first mass seabird deaths from the virus occurred this month, marking an escalation of the outbreak that began after the virus was first detected in migratory seabirds before spreading to native species. South Australian authorities confirmed the nation’s first mammalian detection in the seal, found at the coastal town of Beachport, about 310 km south of Adelaide, the state capital. A fur seal suns itself on harbor steps in front of the Sydney Opera House in Australia on Oct. 2, 2014. Photo: AP The seal, one of an estimated population of 100,000 in the state’s waters, was found dead with no signs of injury, the authorities said. Its death “does not appear to be associated with other sick or dead marine mammals,” Australian Minister for Agriculture, Fisheries and Forestry Julie Collins said in televised remarks. “Once the H5 bird flu is spreading in the natural environment it is not possible to avoid significant losses,” she said. Australia was the last continent to report bird flu, although the virus was detected late last year on the subantarctic territory of Heard Island. More than 13,000 baby seals were killed by the virus on the island, local media reported. Seals are at high risk of contracting the virus because they live near wild birds on rocky outcrops on the coast, South Australia Department of Primary Industries and Regions Chief Veterinary Officer Skye Fruean said. “They would be coming across, no doubt, really high levels of the virus in their environment,” Fruean said in televised remarks. H5N1 has not shown up in Australia’s poultry or agriculture, according to the federal government, which on Friday said that 297 cases had been confirmed nationwide. 印度抗击 H1N1 流感之际,世界卫生组织在整个亚太地区发出 H5N1 警报

As India Battles H1N1, WHO Sounds H5N1 Alert Across Asia-Pacific
新闻简报世界卫生组织(WHO)针对亚太地区H5N1禽流感威胁不断上升​​发出警报,强调需要保持警惕和做好准备。这一警告是在鸟类中 暴发 激增之际发出的,特别是在澳大利亚和新西兰,并强调了“One Health”方法对监测和应对从动物到人类的潜在 暴发 的重要性。虽然 H5N1 可以导致人类​​严重疾病和死亡,但 传播 仍然很少见。世界卫生组织敦促各国加强 监测、实验室能力和数据共享,以及时发现和应对任何人类感染。印度已经在监测季节性流感,并被要求将其 监测 工作与动物和环境卫生部门的工作结合起来。2003年至2025年8月,世界卫生组织报告了25个国家990例人类H5N1病例,其中475人死亡,死亡率很高。然而,一般人群面临的风险仍然很低。世卫组织会议强调了根据 监测 数据及时采取行动的重要性,并呼吁加强合作以优化现有能力并避免重复。
查看新闻五要素
时间
世卫组织警告是在2026年8月25日至27日举行的第19届国家流感中心和流感监测双区域会议期间发布的。世卫组织最新会议结果于2026年8月27日报告。
地点与对象
该警告与整个亚太地区相关,特别提到了澳大利亚和新西兰。印度也在监测季节性流感,并敦促加强 监测 以及与动物和环境卫生当局的协调。
事件
世界卫生组织(WHO)就亚太地区H5N1禽流感威胁不断上升​​发出警报,强调需要保持警惕和做好准备。根据世卫组织的官方警告和会议结果,这是已确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
从2003年到2025年8月,世界卫生组织报告了25个国家的990例人类H5N1病例,其中475人死亡。死亡率为48%,该病毒可导致人类严重呼吸道疾病和死亡。然而,人类 传播 仍然很少见,对普通人群的风险很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织敦促各国加强 监测、实验室能力、基因组测序和数据共享。目前正在对应对措施进行调查,重点是人类、动物和环境卫生部门的准备和协调。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

As India Battles H1N1, WHO Sounds H5N1 Alert Across Asia-Pacific

News Brief
The World Health Organisation (WHO) has issued an alert about the rising threat of H5N1 bird flu in the Asia-Pacific region, emphasizing the need for vigilance and preparedness. This warning comes amid a surge in outbreaks among birds, particularly in Australia and New Zealand, and highlights the importance of the One Health approach to monitor and respond to potential spillovers from animals to humans. While H5N1 can cause severe illness and death in humans, transmission remains rare. The WHO has urged countries to strengthen surveillance, laboratory capacity, and data sharing to detect and respond to any human infections promptly. India, which is already monitoring seasonal influenza, is being called upon to integrate its surveillance efforts with those in animal and environmental health sectors. From 2003 to August 2025, the WHO reported 990 human H5N1 cases across 25 countries, with 475 deaths, indicating a high fatality rate. However, the risk to the general population remains low. The WHO meeting stressed the importance of timely action based on surveillance data and called for greater collaboration to optimize existing capacities and avoid duplication.
Original source text (English)
WHO warns of rising H5N1 bird outbreaks across Asia-Pacific. India is urged to strengthen surveillance and One Health coordination, though human H5N1 transmission remains rare. Just as India is gearing up to tackle a seasonal influenza wave, with H1N1 or swine flu cases drawing attention, the World Health Organisation (WHO) has sounded a fresh warning on another influenza threat — highly pathogenic avian influenza A (H5N1). The warning comes amid a spurt in outbreaks of H5N1 among birds in countries across the Asia-Pacific region, raising concerns over the possibility of the virus spilling over from animals to humans. The WHO has now urged countries to strengthen surveillance and ensure their health systems are ready to detect and respond rapidly to any human infections. The message came after more than 150 influenza experts, laboratory scientists and public-health officials met virtually from August 25 to 27 for the 19th Biregional Meeting of National Influenza Centres and Influenza Surveillance, jointly organised by WHO’s South-East Asia and Western Pacific regional offices. The timing is significant for India, which is already monitoring seasonal influenza. The National Centre for Disease Control (NCDC) maintains dedicated surveillance and technical guidance for seasonal influenza, including H1N1, and its latest update was issued on August 11. But H5N1 is a different threat. While seasonal influenza viruses such as H1N1 circulate primarily among humans, H5N1 is an avian influenza virus that primarily affects birds and can occasionally infect humans, particularly following exposure to infected animals or contaminated environments. WHO says zoonotic influenza viruses can cause illness ranging from mild flu-like symptoms to severe respiratory disease and death. The latest WHO meeting placed particular emphasis on the spread of highly pathogenic H5N1 in birds in the Pacific and recent incursions reported in Australia and New Zealand. Swine Flu Accounts For 98% Of Tested Cases In India, No Deaths: ICMR H1N1 Cases in Delhi: Swine Flu Infections Rise In National Capital, Health Ministry Urges Early Medical Attention H1N1 Cases Rise, But No Need To Panic; Doctors Urge Vaccination For High-Risk Groups As JP Nadda Reviews Preparedness Centre Deploys National Joint Outbreak Response Team Amid Chandipura Virus Outbreak WHO stressed that the response cannot be left to the health sector alone. Animal health, human health and environmental authorities need to work together under the One Health approach to detect potential spillovers early and contain them before they become a wider public-health threat. “Australia and New Zealand’s recent experience is a sharp reminder that vigilance cannot lapse,” said Dr Saia Ma’u Piukala, WHO Regional Director for the Western Pacific, stressing the importance of coordination between human, animal and environmental health authorities. WHO, along with the Food and Agriculture Organisation, UN Environment Programme, World Organisation for Animal Health and Pacific Community, has also undertaken a Rapid Joint One Health Risk Assessment to assess the risk of H5N1 entering Pacific Island countries and identify preparedness measures. For India, the warning is relevant because influenza surveillance cannot operate in isolation from developments in animal populations. Early detection depends on being able to identify unusual infections, characterise viruses and share information rapidly between human and animal health systems. India showcased its own surveillance experience at the WHO meeting, highlighting the integration of influenza surveillance into the Integrated Health Information Platform. The system provides near-real-time data for analysing influenza trends and supporting faster outbreak detection and response. The WHO's latest global respiratory-virus update also shows that influenza activity is not uniformly high worldwide. However, elevated influenza positivity has been reported in parts of South and South-East Asia and several other regions, reinforcing the need for continued surveillance. The distinction between H1N1 and H5N1 is therefore important. A rise in seasonal H1N1 does not mean H5N1 is spreading among people. WHO continues to describe avian influenza viruses as distinct from seasonal human influenza viruses, with human transmission remaining an important point of surveillance rather than something to assume from animal outbreaks alone. The WHO meeting repeatedly returned to one message: collecting data is not enough unless it leads to timely action. “Surveillance data only matter when they change what public health systems do,” said Dr Gina Samaan, WHO Regional Emergency Director for the Western Pacific. The region is consequently being urged to strengthen laboratory capacity, genomic sequencing, timely sharing of influenza viruses and data, and the ability to rapidly characterise unusual strains. The broader concern is preparedness. H5N1 has been causing unprecedented levels of disease and deaths among wild birds and poultry in several parts of the world since the emergence of the H5 clade 2.3.4.4b viruses in 2020. Human infections remain rare, but when they occur, they can be severe. For India, the immediate public-health priority remains keeping a close watch on seasonal influenza and ensuring appropriate diagnosis and treatment. But the WHO alert is also a reminder that influenza preparedness cannot stop at human hospitals. “Our strength is in our network – in sharing expertise, information and capacities, and in supporting one another to be better prepared for the threats ahead,” said Dr Nilesh Buddha, Officer-in-Charge for the WHO Regional Office for South-East Asia. “Where expertise exists in one country, we should facilitate its transfer and application across the region; where specialised capacities are available, we should strengthen pathways for countries to access them.” Dr Buddha also stressed the importance of coordinated investments, resource mobilisation and strategic prioritisation at a time when resources are increasingly constrained. He called for greater collaboration to make optimal use of existing capacities, maximise impact and avoid duplication. From 2003 to August 2025, the WHO has reported 990 human H5N1 cases across 25 countries, including 475 deaths – a 48% fatality rate. In humans, H5N1 infection can resemble a severe bout of influenza, with symptoms such as high fever, cough, sore throat and muscle aches, while some infected people may remain symptom-free. Although the risk to the general population remains low, health authorities are closely monitoring the virus for any changes that could increase its ability to spread among humans, according to reports
View five news elements
Time
The WHO warning was issued during the 19th Biregional Meeting of National Influenza Centres and Influenza Surveillance, which took place from August 25 to 27, 2026. The latest WHO meeting results were reported on August 27, 2026.
Location and population
The warning is relevant across the Asia-Pacific region, with specific mention of Australia and New Zealand. India is also monitoring seasonal influenza and is urged to strengthen surveillance and coordination with animal and environmental health authorities.
Event
The World Health Organisation (WHO) issued an alert about the rising threat of H5N1 bird flu in the Asia-Pacific region, emphasizing the need for vigilance and preparedness. This is a confirmed event based on WHO's official warning and meeting outcomes.
Scale, impact and risk
From 2003 to August 2025, the WHO reported 990 human H5N1 cases across 25 countries, with 475 deaths. The fatality rate is 48%, and the virus can cause severe respiratory disease and death in humans. However, human transmission remains rare, and the risk to the general population is low.
Response, status and uncertainty
WHO has urged countries to strengthen surveillance, laboratory capacity, genomic sequencing, and data sharing. The response is under investigation, with a focus on preparedness and coordination across human, animal, and environmental health sectors.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳大利亚大陆哺乳动物首例禽流感死亡事件是一个严峻的里程碑。我们知道这会变得多么糟糕 - 对话

First bird flu death in an Australian mainland mammal is a grim milestone. We know how bad this can get - The Conversation
新闻简报这篇文章报道了澳大利亚大陆首例确诊的感染 H5N1 禽流感的长鼻海豹死亡事件,死因是在南澳大利亚石灰岩海岸的海滩港发现的。这标志着正在进行的 H5N1 死亡率 的重大发展,因为该病毒此前曾在阿根廷和南乔治亚岛的海豹中造成严重的 死亡率。海豹可能是通过接触受感染的鸟类或受污染的环境而感染病毒的。虽然没有证据表明澳大利亚存在更广泛的海洋哺乳动物 暴发,但快速传播的潜力凸显,特别是考虑到密集的海豹栖息地以及与受感染鸟类共享的栖息地。文章强调,需要针对麦格理岛上的澳大利亚海狮和南象海豹等脆弱物种采取紧急 监测 和有针对性的 疫苗接种 策略研究。然而,目前尚未在澳大利亚批准使用 疫苗,并且导致 Beachport 感染的确切病毒谱系仍不确定。这种情况凸显了 H5N1 对海洋哺乳动物的风险以及监测其传播以防止濒危种群遭受灾难性损失的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-23,事件发生在 2026 年。文章引用了 2023 年和 2025 年的过去事件。
地点与对象
该事件发生在南澳大利亚,特别是在石灰岩海岸的海滩港。受影响的种群包括长鼻海狗、澳大利亚海狗和澳大利亚海狮,特别是南澳大利亚的海狮。
事件
南澳大利亚海滩港发现一只长鼻海豹死亡,这是澳大利亚大陆哺乳动物中首例确诊的 H5N1 禽流感病例。这是《对话》根据科学发现报道的。
规模、影响与风险
注意到一只海豹的死亡,但文章强调了更广泛的 暴发 的潜力,引用了阿根廷和南乔治亚岛海豹中过去的 H5N1 暴发。它还提到了澳大利亚海狮等濒危物种的脆弱性以及紧急 监测 的需要。
应对、状态与不确定性
敦促澳大利亚实施紧急 监测 并调查针对脆弱海豹种群的针对性 疫苗接种 策略。然而,疫苗 尚未获批在澳大利亚使用,并且感染海滩港海豹的确切病毒谱系尚不清楚。文章强调需要进一步研究和监测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

First bird flu death in an Australian mainland mammal is a grim milestone. We know how bad this can get - The Conversation

News Brief
The article reports the first confirmed death of an H5N1 bird flu-infected long-nosed fur seal on Australian mainland, found dead at Beachport on South Australia’s Limestone Coast. This marks a critical development in the ongoing H5N1 outbreak, as the virus has previously caused severe mortality in seals in Argentina and South Georgia. The seal likely contracted the virus through contact with infected birds or contaminated environments. While there is no evidence of a wider marine mammal outbreak in Australia, the potential for rapid spread is highlighted, especially given the dense seal colonies and shared habitats with infected birds. The article stresses the need for urgent surveillance and research into targeted vaccination strategies for vulnerable species like the Australian sea lion and southern elephant seals on Macquarie Island. However, no approved vaccines are currently available for use in Australia, and the exact viral lineage responsible for the Beachport infection remains undetermined. The situation underscores the risks of H5N1 to marine mammals and the importance of monitoring its spread to prevent catastrophic losses in endangered populations.

We know how bad this can get Published: August 23, 2026 4:16am EDT Australia has crossed another grim threshold in its H5N1 bird flu outbreak. For the first time, the virus has infected an Australian mainland mammal: a long-nosed fur seal found dead at Beachport on South Australia’s Limestone Coast, about a four-hour drive southeast of Adelaide. This is a devastating development, although sadly not an unexpected one. H5N1 is already circulating in wild seabirds along the Limestone Coast, with many infected greater crested terns found at Beachport itself. This seal likely caught the virus through contact with either infected birds or the environment they share. As of today, there is no evidence that it is part of a wider marine mammal outbreak. One infected seal is not a seal outbreak. But we know from overseas just how quickly that can change. We know how bad this can get When H5N1 reached southern elephant seals in Argentina in 2023 more than 17,000 pups died. At one intensively monitored colony, mortality eventually reached 95%. Genetic evidence strongly supported transmission between mammals, rather than thousands of seals independently becoming infected from birds. The devastation followed the virus into the Southern Ocean. On South Georgia island (east of the Falkland Islands), the number of breeding female southern elephant seals fell by 47% between 2022 and 2024, following the arrival of H5N1. Dead elephant seals in South Georgia, seen in early 2025 during the bird flu outbreak on the island. Jane Younger Then, at Australia’s Heard Island (about 1,700 km north of Antarctica), an estimated 13,359 elephant seal pups are believed to have died during the 2025 outbreak. Antarctic fur seals have also been hit extremely hard. Field observations suggest they may have suffered the greatest number of deaths of any species, although the scale of the outbreaks makes a reliable total impossible. These are not isolated animal deaths. For already threatened species – such as the Australian sea lion, the Antarctic fur seal and the southern elephant seal – losses on this scale can push species closer to extinction. What is a long-nosed fur seal – and what happens next? Long-nosed fur seals, formerly known as New Zealand fur seals, occur in Western Australia, South Australia, Tasmania and New Zealand. The South Australian population is estimated at around 100,000 individuals. We do not know exactly how the Beachport seal became infected, but infection could occur through direct contact with infected birds or other seals, or exposure to contaminated beaches or water. Australia is also home to Australian fur seals and the endangered Australian sea lion, a species found nowhere else on Earth. Its entire population is estimated at fewer than 12,000 animals, around 85% of them in South Australia. H5N1-infected birds have already been found at Seal Bay on Kangaroo Island, while mass bird deaths are occurring at the South Australian island group The Pages – both important Australian sea lion breeding locations. Australia’s Macquarie Island (about 1,500 km southeast of Tasmania) is also home to southern elephant seals and endangered Antarctic fur seals – both species that have faced huge losses from H5N1 in other parts of the world. Seal populations now need urgent surveillance. We can’t stop H5N1 spreading through a colony, but we need to know where it is, how quickly it is spreading and which populations are being hardest hit. Why are seals so vulnerable? Seals gather on the coast in dense colonies, in close contact with each other and in contaminated ground and water, creating opportunities for rapid transmission. But the biology of the virus matters too. H5N1 is fundamentally a bird virus, but the lineage that spread through marine mammals in South America acquired mutations that improve its ability to replicate in the cells of mammals. Our recent research (yet to have been peer reviewed) found that H5N1 continued to evolve as it spread through seals, including more changes that help it replicate in mammals. But severe outbreaks in seals also occurred without these changes, suggesting seals may be particularly vulnerable to the virus. We do not yet know which viral lineage infected the Beachport seal. Rapid genome sequencing will be important to determine whether it carries the mammalian-adaptive mutations seen overseas. Australia needs a seal plan now There are currently no commercially available H5 vaccines suitable for mammals available in Australia. But there is promising overseas work. In the United States, researchers tested a vaccine – originally developed for dairy cattle – in northern elephant seals. The vaccine was well tolerated and produced a clear, measurable antibody response. Researchers then extended the trial to endangered Hawaiian monk seals in rehabilitation. This does not yet amount to a vaccination program for free-ranging seals, and the vaccine does not have regulatory approval for use in Australia. Vaccinating every seal colony would also be impossible. Fur seals typically enter the water as soon as people approach, making vaccination infeasible for this species. But Australia should urgently investigate whether targeted vaccination could protect select populations where intervention is both feasible and worthwhile – particularly endangered Australian sea lions, and southern elephant seals on Macquarie Island, which remains an enormously important Australian breeding population. Vaccination will never stop H5N1 spreading through wildlife. But it may help prevent catastrophic losses in the species we can reach. The Beachport seal is one animal – for now. But this virus has already shown us what can happen next. Australia should use that warning while we still have time. Please do not touch sick or dead birds or marine mammals. Record the location, take photographs from a safe distance, keep pets away, and report the sighting to the Emergency Animal Disease Hotline on 1800 675 888. Avian flu H5N1 Avian flu Bird flu Bird flu outbreak H5N1 avian influenza Avian influeza Want to write? B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

海狗的死亡标志着澳大利亚大陆首例因 H​​5 禽流感病毒传播而死亡的哺乳动物 - 卫报

Fur seal’s death marks first Australian mainland mammal fatality from spreading H5 bird flu strain - The Guardian
新闻简报南澳大利亚海滩港发现的一只长鼻毛皮海豹的 H5 禽流感检测呈阳性,这是澳大利亚大陆首例确诊的哺乳动物死于该病毒的病例。《卫报》于 2026 年 8 月 23 日报道了这一事件,并得到了南澳大利亚州政府的证实。H5禽流感已在全国范围内蔓延,已记录298起事件,其中南澳大利亚州183起,维多利亚州86起,新南威尔士州、西澳大利亚州、塔斯马尼亚州和昆士兰州有其他事件。该病毒已在近海岛屿引发大规模 死亡率 事件,超过 1,000 只大凤头燕鸥受到影响。联邦农业部长朱莉·柯林斯表示,病毒向哺乳动物传播并不意外,并警告称可能会造成重大损失。人类感染 传播 的风险仍然很低,但当局敦促人们避免接触病鸟或死鸟并进行报告。南澳大利亚环境部长 Emily Bourke 正在考虑将来自美国的实验性 疫苗 用于哺乳动物,但尚未获得批准。袋鼠岛的小企鹅身上也发现了这种病毒,一些企鹅的死亡被认为是死于 H5 禽流感。该病毒向长鼻海狗和澳大利亚海狮等海洋哺乳动物的传播引起了人们对潜在灭绝事件的担忧,因为这些物种面临着非常高的风险。政府正在密切关注事态发展,并正在努力实施应对策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
周三,这只海豹在海滩港被发现死亡,该事件于 2026 年 8 月 23 日被报道。该病毒一直在澳大利亚传播,南澳大利亚和其他州发生了大规模 死亡率 事件。
地点与对象
该事件发生在南澳大利亚,特别是在石灰岩海岸的海滩港。受影响的种群包括长鼻海狗和濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮,它们感染该病毒的风险非常高。
事件
一只长鼻海豹被发现死亡,并检测出 H5 禽流感阳性,这是澳大利亚大陆首例确诊的哺乳动物因该病毒死亡。这是《卫报》报道的,并得到了南澳大利亚政府的证实。
规模、影响与风险
H5禽流感已在澳大利亚各地引发298起事件,其中南澳大利亚州有183起,维多利亚州有86起,其他州也有其他事件。人类感染 传播 的风险仍然很低,但该病毒正在 野生鸟类 和海洋哺乳动物之间传播,可能对野生动物种群造成重大损失。
应对、状态与不确定性
南澳大利亚州政府正在与联邦政府合作评估实验性 疫苗 在哺乳动物中的使用情况。部分企鹅的死因尚无法确定,进一步的检测正在进行中。人们承认病毒传播的风险,但尚未采取明确的措施来预防。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Fur seal’s death marks first Australian mainland mammal fatality from spreading H5 bird flu strain - The Guardian

News Brief
A long-nosed fur seal found dead at Beachport in South Australia has tested positive for H5 bird flu, marking the first confirmed mammal fatality from the virus on the Australian mainland. The event was reported by The Guardian on August 23, 2026, and confirmed by the South Australian government. The H5 bird flu has been spreading across the country, with 298 events recorded, including 183 in South Australia, 86 in Victoria, and others in New South Wales, Western Australia, Tasmania, and Queensland. The virus has already caused mass mortality events in offshore islands, with over 1,000 greater crested terns affected. The federal agriculture minister, Julie Collins, stated that the spread of the virus to mammals is not unexpected and warned of significant losses. The risk of human transmission remains low, but authorities are urging people to avoid contact with sick or dead birds and report them. The South Australian environment minister, Emily Bourke, is considering the use of an experimental vaccine from the United States for mammals, though it is not yet approved. The virus has also been detected in little penguins on Kangaroo Island, with some deaths assumed to be from H5 bird flu. The spread of the virus to marine mammals like long-nosed fur seals and Australian sea lions raises concerns about potential extinction events, as these species are at very high risk. The government is monitoring the situation closely and is working to implement a response strategy.
Original source text (English)

Photograph: David C Tomlinson/Getty Images Fur seal’s death marks first Australian mainland mammal fatality from spreading H5 bird flu strain Seal found dead at Beachport in South Australia presages ‘significant losses’, minister warns Follow our Australia news live blog for latest updates Get our breaking news email, free app or daily news podcast A long-nosed fur seal has died from H5 bird flu in South Australia, marking the first confirmed case in a mammal on the Australian mainland. The seal was found dead at Beachport on Wednesday on the state’s Limestone Coast. It marks a fresh escalation in the spread of the virus days after the South Australian premier, Peter Malinauskas, announced three mass mortality events on offshore islands involving more than 1,000 greater crested terns. Sign up for the Breaking News Australia email The federal agriculture minister, Julie Collins, said the seal death was “not unexpected” as the virus begins to spread through the country. Speaking in Hobart on Sunday, Collins said the “distressing” news of the first confirmed case of a mammal on the mainland was anticipated and that it was “not possible to avoid significant losses”. “An infection of this individual animal is likely to have occurred due to exposure to infected wild birds or the environment that they share,” she said. “We can’t stop it spreading in terms of, particularly, in wild birds, and where we see more dead wild birds, we do expect to see increased reports of marine mammals.” Collins said there had been 298 events across the country, with 183 in South Australia, 86 in Victoria, 11 in New South Wales, 10 in Western Australia, seven in Tasmania and one in Queensland. The risk of human transmission remains low, but people are being urged to avoid sick or dead birds and report them to birdflu.gov.au. ‘Devastating’: Western Australia fears a black swan moment as bird flu threatens flagship species Read more The long-nosed fur seal was found dead with no signs of injury and reported to an official hotline by a member of the public on Wednesday Testing of samples from the seal later confirmed the H5 bird flu detection. The highly pathogenic strain of bird flu has killed millions of birds globally and crossed over into mammal species, including elephant seals. Long-nosed fur seals and the endangered Australian sea lion are among the mammal species the federal government has assessed as facing very high risk from the virus. Jack Gough, the chief executive of the Invasive Species Council, said the virus spreading to Australia’s sea lion population could be an “extinction level event”. “We are still just at the early stages of this devastating virus, and the bad news is going to keep coming for our wildlife as it spreads around the continent and between species,” he said. The SA government has also confirmed a little penguin found at Kangaroo Island this week tested positive for H5 bird flu. The SA’s environment minister, Emily Bourke, said the state would work with the federal government to assess whether an experimental vaccine from the United States could be used for mammals in Australia. “It is not an off-the-shelf vaccine that’s been approved, and we have to be taking into consideration the risk [of undertaking] the vaccination program in our mammals,” she said. Australian Marine Conservation Society campaign director, Alexia Wellbelove, urged the federal government to create a $200m wildlife resilience fund for threatened species at risk of mass mortalities or local extinctions. “Government preparedness funding got us this far, but it was never designed to manage active spread across multiple species and states,” Wellbelove said. Elsewhere, the Tasmanian government said 51 little penguins found on the state’s King Island were “assumed to” have died from H5 bird flu, but the deaths would not be declared a mass mortality event. The state’s natural resources and environment department said a sample taken from one penguin found at the Port of Grassy tested positive for H5 bird flu, while a sample from a second penguin was inconclusive. Wes Ford, the state’s incident controller for its bird flu response, said both samples had been sent to the Australian Centre for Disease Preparedness for further testing. “The little penguin mortality event is not considered a mass mortality event, and the condition of the remaining deceased birds meant they were unsuitable for testing,” he said. “As a result, the cause of death could not be confirmed, although it may be assumed to have been from bird flu. “Our teams continue to monitor reports of sick and dead birds closely, with results from surveillance informing our response.” Explore more on these topics Bird flu South Australia news Share Reuse this content Most viewed Senator calls for criminal investigation of RFK Jr after Guardian report Dolly Parton obituary Warning of fresh danger from rising lake water in Nepal – as it happened Ukraine war briefing: Missiles shot down during long Russian attack wave Jon Ossoff went low on Natalie Harp. News Brief
The article reports that H5N1 Avian Flu has spread to every Australian state, and the CSIRO is actively monitoring its transmission. The information is based on a general update from the CSIRO, and the article does not provide further details on the impact or risk associated with the outbreak. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

更智能的监测能否帮助家禽养殖场更早感染禽流感? - AgFunderNews

Can smarter surveillance help poultry farms catch bird flu earlier? - AgFunderNews
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 25 日,AgFunderNews 报道 Alveo Technologies 和 AgriNerds 合作开发禽流感预警系统。该系统结合了 AgriNerds 的水禽警报网络(该网络使用雷达、卫星图像和遥测技术来预测 野生鸟类 的感染风险)与 Alveo 的便携式分子诊断平台(能够在 45 分钟内检测禽流感)。其目的是使家禽养殖户能够采取主动措施,例如限制活动和改变通风,以限制农场之间的 传播。Alveo 的测试采用分子扩增技术,目前正在接受美国农业部的评估,预计将于 2026 年第四季度获得批准。该系统旨在通过将农场测试结果与环境数据相结合来提供实时警报并改善疾病管理,从而可能减少大规模扑杀的需要。然而,早期检测的价值仍然存在争议,因为受感染的鸟类可能仍然需要扑杀,而且该系统尚未广泛使用。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 8 月 25 日 | Elaine Watson Agtech Deeptech Livestock/animal ag 野禽可以通过唾液、鼻腔分泌物和粪便传播禽流感病毒,感染附近的家禽养殖场。
地点与对象
美国,商业家禽设施遍布 25 个州。
事件
Alveo Technologies 和 AgriNerds 之间的合作旨在利用预测模型和快速农场测试开发禽流感早期预警系统。该系统旨在根据野禽活动和分子诊断的数据,在 暴发 升级之前检测并遏制它们。
规模、影响与风险
该系统将 AgriNerds 的水禽警报网络与 Alveo 的便携式分子诊断平台相结合。它可以在 45 分钟内高精度地检测出禽流感,通过早期遏制措施可能减少大规模扑杀的需要。从 野生鸟类 到家禽养殖场的禽流感 传播 风险被强调为主要问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
该合作伙伴关系正在获得美国农业部和州兽医的实验批准。Alveo 的测试目前正在美国进行评估,预计将于 2026 年第四季度获得批准。该系统旨在通过数据驱动的方法提供实时警报并改善疾病管理。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Can smarter surveillance help poultry farms catch bird flu earlier? - AgFunderNews

News Brief
On August 25, 2026, AgFunderNews reported that Alveo Technologies and AgriNerds have partnered to develop an early warning system for avian flu. This system combines AgriNerds’ Waterfowl Alert Network, which uses radar, satellite imagery, and telemetry to predict infection risk from wild birds, with Alveo’s portable molecular diagnostics platform capable of detecting avian flu in 45 minutes. The aim is to enable poultry farmers to take proactive measures, such as restricting movements and modifying ventilation, to limit farm-to-farm transmission. Alveo’s test, which uses molecular amplification technology, is currently under USDA evaluation and is expected to be approved by Q4 2026. The system is designed to provide real-time alerts and improve disease management by integrating on-farm test results with environmental data, potentially reducing the need for large-scale culling. However, the value of early detection remains debated, as infected birds may still require culling, and the system is not yet in widespread use.
Original source text (English)
Menu About Us Newsletter Investor Q&A Agtech FoodTech Deeptech Video Can smarter surveillance help poultry farms catch bird flu earlier? August 25, 2026 | Elaine Watson Agtech Deeptech Livestock/animal ag Wildfowl can shed avian flu viruses in their saliva, nasal secretions, and feces, infecting nearby poultry farms. Image credit: istock/arlutz73 Rapid on-farm pathogen testing firm Alveo Technologies has teamed up with wildfowl surveillance specialist AgriNerds to create an early warning system for avian flu it claims could dramatically improve poultry farmers’ ability to detect and contain outbreaks before they escalate. The partnership combines AgriNerds’ Waterfowl Alert Network—which uses radar, satellite imagery, telemetry (radio tags to track wild birds), and other data to predict where infection risk is highest from wild birds—with Alveo’s portable molecular diagnostics platform, which can detect avian flu in 45 minutes. The aim is to find a middle ground between impractical, round-the-clock “super-surveillance” and waiting for birds to show symptoms before testing and then waiting another two to three days for PCR lab results—by which point the virus may already have spread within and beyond the farm. Instead, producers could step up testing when AgriNerds’ model signals that migratory bird activity or other factors have increased the likelihood of infection, Alveo president Erik Tyrrell-Knott told AgFunderNews. Data generated by Alveo’s tests—which are already on the market in several countries and on track to secure USDA approval later this year—can in turn feed back into AgriNerds’ models and potentially make subsequent risk predictions more precise. “Right now, if egg production goes down, if the birds appear lethargic, or the mortality rate goes up, farmers have a decision to make,” said Tyrrell-Knott. “It might be bird flu. It might be something else. Are you going to incur a lot of expense and take all these [extra] biosecurity measures flying blind, or are you going to wait days [for lab test results] and it’s business as usual [in the meantime]? “We’ve got two 21st century technologies combining to provide informed, intelligent, data-driven surveillance guidance, which is something that is totally missing in the current landscape.” AgriNerds has developed a predictive tool for determining waterfowl abundance in close proximity to commercial poultry. Image credit: AgriNerds Grading risk for dynamic surveillance Waterfowl are a major reservoir of bird flu, creating a risk when infected birds fly over or roost near poultry farms. AgriNerds combines multiple data sources to predict waterfowl abundance close to individual poultry facilities and classifies risk as low, moderate, or high. While seasonal migration patterns are broadly predictable, the risk surrounding individual farms can vary significantly from one year to the next as weather, crops, water availability and habitat change, said AgriNerds founder Maurice Pitesky, an associate professor at the UC Davis Weill school of veterinary medicine. Generally, he said, wild birds are looking for a food source and a water source. And both can be subject to change, for example should a nearby farmer plant new or different crops, or should heavy rain create an environment that might attract waterfowl. The network now makes daily predictions across the country relative to 160,000+ commercial poultry facilities, said Pitesky, who says AgriNerds’ data is now used by 1,500+ farms across 25 states. “The farm doesn’t change location, but the risk does change.” Users could, for example, choose only to receive alerts when a farm enters the highest-risk category. “The vet wakes up and is notified in real time: You’ve got a red day today.” What does earlier detection actually change? But what is the value of early detection if infected flocks must ultimately be destroyed? For infected birds, says Tyrrell-Knott, there’s not much you can do. But if you catch bird flu early, you can restrict movements of employees, feed and waste trucks, and take other measures aimed at limiting farm-to-farm transmission. Farms can also modify ventilation practices designed to limit the dispersal of potentially contaminated material. This is particularly important because infection does not only move from wild birds into poultry operations but in about 30% of cases is spread from farm to farm through people, vehicles, equipment or airborne material, he claimed. Pitesky said Alveo’s results could also add much more granular disease data to AgriNerds’ risk model. Rather than relying on bird flu detections reported at county level, it could potentially incorporate geotagged test results from individual farms and combine these with wind direction and other environmental information. “If you have an on-farm test that comes back positive, we can understand where feather dander might be traveling from infected facilities and warn farms that are downwind.” Longer term, more rapid testing combined with more detailed intelligence on bird movements could reduce the overall number of birds that have to be culled in a large facility if early detection means the virus can be contained within one barn, said Tyrrell-Knott. “We believe in future with our accuracy, our sensitivity, the rapidity and trustworthiness of our results, that you could start to partially depopulate some of these complexes when you’ve got 13 barns with two million birds on a complex, instead of having to cull all the birds.” Alveo is deploying “basically the same molecular amplification technology [used in a laboratory],” says the firm. “But we’ve reduced it to the size of a cell phone and made it rugged, easy to use and affordable.” Image credit: Alveo Technologies Testing asymptomatic flocks Alveo’s platform uses molecular amplification rather than the lateral-flow technology used in rapid strip tests, which are not hugely reliable, claimed Tyrrell-Knott. “It’s rapid, but 30-40% of the time they miss the virus, so you get a false negative.” Alveo, by contrast, is deploying “basically the same molecular amplification technology [used in a laboratory],” said Tyrrell-Knott. “But we’ve reduced it to the size of a cell phone and made it rugged, easy to use and affordable.” The tech can distinguish between H5 and H7 subtypes of avian influenza at 96-99% accuracy, approaching what you’d get from PCR tests in a lab, results from which can take 2-3 days to arrive, he claimed. In the US, the Alveo test currently being evaluated uses oropharyngeal swabs, while outside the US Alveo also uses cloacal swabs from the birds’ rear end, says the firm. Building an early-warning network The broader opportunity is to shift bird flu management further upstream, from responding to outbreaks after they occur toward identifying when farms are exposed to unusually high external risk and testing accordingly, said Pitesky. “In this way, agriculture can move past reactive disease management to adopt a proactive, risk-informed approach.” Tyrrell-Knott added: “We’ve been approved for experimental use by the USDA and state vets and we believe we’re on track for approval in Q4 of this year as the first on farm molecular bird flu test in the US. We’re not allowed to pre-sell but we spoke at a big poultry conference in Texas a few weeks ago and we were mobbed.” Share this article LinkedInXFacebookEmail Sign up for our weekly newsletter. Time
August 25, 2026 | Elaine Watson Agtech Deeptech Livestock/animal ag Wildfowl can shed avian flu viruses in their saliva, nasal secretions, and feces, infecting nearby poultry farms.
Location and population
United States, commercial poultry facilities across 25 states.
Event
A partnership between Alveo Technologies and AgriNerds aims to develop an early warning system for avian flu using predictive models and rapid on-farm testing. This system is designed to detect and contain outbreaks before they escalate, based on data from wildfowl activity and molecular diagnostics.
Scale, impact and risk
The system combines AgriNerds’ Waterfowl Alert Network with Alveo’s portable molecular diagnostics platform. It can detect avian flu in 45 minutes with high accuracy, potentially reducing the need for large-scale culling by enabling early containment measures. The risk of avian flu transmission from wild birds to poultry farms is highlighted as a major concern.
Response, status and uncertainty
The partnership is in the process of experimental approval by the USDA and state veterinarians. Alveo’s test is currently being evaluated in the US and is expected to be approved by Q4 2026. The system is designed to provide real-time alerts and improve disease management through data-driven approaches.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳大利亚确认当地哺乳动物出现H5N1禽流感病例 - DW.com

Australia confirms H5N1 bird flu case in local mammal - DW.com
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 23 日,澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物(特别是长鼻海狗)感染 H5N1 禽流感病例。该海豹被发现死亡于南澳大利亚州海滩港,距阿德莱德约 310 公里。当局称该病毒向哺乳动物的传播令人担忧,但并不意外,并指出 H5N1 病毒一直在自然环境中传播。政府启动了鸟类 疫苗接种 计划,截至周五报告了 297 例确诊病例,没有蔓延到家禽或农业。联邦农业部长朱莉·柯林斯强调,该病毒在海洋哺乳动物中的存在是一种自然进展,特别是在 野生鸟类 感染率高的地区。该病例凸显了 H5N1 禽流感的持续威胁以及持续监测和预防措施的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
澳大利亚于 2026 年 8 月 23 日确认当地哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感病例。本月早些时候,在南澳大利亚海滩港的一只长鼻海豹身上发现了该病毒。
地点与对象
该病例在南澳大利亚沿海小镇海滩港确诊,距离阿德莱德约310公里。受影响的种群包括长鼻海狗,该州水域内估计有 100,000 只海狗。
事件
澳大利亚在哺乳动物体内检测到病毒后,确认了首例感染 H5N1 禽流感的哺乳动物,特别是长鼻海狗。这是澳大利亚当局报告的已证实的事件。
规模、影响与风险
受感染的海豹被发现已经死亡,没有受伤的迹象。当局表示,H5N1 病毒向哺乳动物的传播令人担忧,但并不意外。该病毒尚未传播到家禽或农业,截至周五已有 297 例确诊病例。政府启动了鸟类疫苗接种计划。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚当局正在积极调查 H5N1 禽流感的传播情况,包括其对野生动物的影响。政府已启动鸟类 疫苗接种 计划并正在监测情况。该病例已通过检测得到证实,目前尚未报告有关传播到其他哺乳动物或人类的进一步细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Australia confirms H5N1 bird flu case in local mammal - DW.com

News Brief
Australia confirmed its first case of H5N1 bird flu in a mammal, specifically a long-nosed fur seal, on August 23, 2026. The seal was found dead in Beachport, South Australia, approximately 310 kilometers from Adelaide. The authorities described the spread of the virus to mammals as concerning but not unexpected, noting that H5N1 has been spreading in the natural environment. The government has initiated a bird vaccination program and reported 297 confirmed cases as of Friday, with no spread to poultry or agriculture. Federal Agriculture Minister Julie Collins emphasized that the virus's presence in marine mammals is a natural progression, especially in areas with high infection rates in wild birds. The case highlights the ongoing threat of H5N1 bird flu and the need for continued monitoring and preventive measures.
Original source text (English)
You need to enable JavaScript to run this app. Skip to content Skip to main menu Skip to more DW sites Latest videos Latest audio Regions Africa Asia Europe Latin America Middle East North America Germany Topics Climate Equality Health Human Rights Migration Technology Categories Business Science Environment Culture Sports In focus Iran warUkraineGerman car industry Latest audioLatest videos Live TV Advertisement HealthAustralia Australia confirms H5N1 bird flu case in local mammal Mahima Kapoor with Reuters 08/23/2026August 23, 2026 Australian authorities said the deadly virus's spread to mammals was "concerning" but "not unexpected." https://p.dw.com/p/5JGde Copy link Australia has been grappling with bird flu since JuneImage: Phillip Island Nature Parks/AFP Advertisement Australia confirmed its first case of H5N1 bird flu in a mammal on Sunday, after it found the deadly virus in a long-nosed fur seal. Authorities in the state of South Australia said they found the virus in the seal in the coastal town of Beachport, about 310 kilometers (192 miles) from the state capital, Adelaide. Austrialia has been combating the highly pathogenic influenza after it arrived in the country in June. Earlier this month it reported its first mass seabird deaths, marking an escalation. What did authorities have to say? There are an estimated 100,000 long-nosed fur seals in the state's waters. The infected individual was found dead with no signs of injury, authorities said. Its death "does not appear to be associated with other sick or dead marine mammals," Federal Agriculture Minister Julie Collins said in televised remarks. "Once the H5 bird flu is spreading in the natural environment it is not possible to avoid significant losses," Collins added. "Although this is a concerning development, it is not unexpected for us to start to see infection in other wildlife, particularly in marine mammals, particularly in areas where there are high infection rates in wild birds," Collins was quoted by the Australian Broadcasting Corporation as saying. Australia began vaccinating its birds against the deadly virus earlier this monthImage: Phillip Island Nature Parks/AFP The virus has not spread to Australia's poultry or agriculture, according to the government, which counted 297 confirmed cases as of Friday. Earlier in August, the government said it would begin a bird vaccination program based on the species' vulnerability to the virus and conservation impact. Australia confirmed the H5N1 bird flu case in a local mammal on August 23, 2026. The virus was found in a long-nosed fur seal in Beachport, South Australia, earlier this month.
Location and population
The case was confirmed in Beachport, a coastal town in South Australia, approximately 310 kilometers from Adelaide. The affected population includes long-nosed fur seals, with an estimated 100,000 in the state's waters.
Event
Australia confirmed its first case of H5N1 bird flu in a mammal, specifically a long-nosed fur seal, after detecting the virus in the animal. This is a confirmed event reported by Australian authorities.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

美国农业部确认家禽和奶牛中出现新的 H5N1 禽流感病例

USDA confirms new H5N1 avian flu cases in poultry, dairy cattle
新闻简报美国农业部 (USDA) 确认商业家禽和奶牛中出现新的 H5N1 禽流感病例,最新更新于 2026 年 8 月 27 日报告。在南达科他州,埃德蒙兹县有 29,700 只禽类受到 暴发 影响,布鲁尔县另有 2,800 只禽类受到影响。此外,德克萨斯州和犹他州的三头奶牛群被证实感染H5N1禽流感病毒。今年已有 85 个牛群感染 H5N1,而去年同期为 171 个,2024 年为 917 个。美国农业部正在积极监测这些 暴发,但没有提供有关具体应对措施或风险评估的进一步细节。暴发 强调了 H5N1 在牲畜中的持续传播,引发了人们对动物健康和食品安全潜在影响的担忧。
查看新闻五要素
时间
美国农业部动植物健康检疫局(APHIS)在过去30天内确认了新的H5N1禽流感病例,最新更新日期为2026-08-27。
地点与对象
暴发 在南达科他州、德克萨斯州和犹他州的商业家禽和奶牛中得到证实,特别提到了南达科他州的埃德蒙兹县和布鲁尔县,以及德克萨斯州和犹他州的奶牛群。
事件
美国农业部确认在家禽和奶牛中出现新的 H5N1 禽流感病例,其中奶牛中确认检测到 3 例,南达科他州的家禽养殖场中检测到大型 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
So far this year, 85 cattle herds have had confirmed H5N1 infections. In South Dakota, 29,700 birds were affected in one outbreak, and 2,800 in another. Last year, APHIS tracked 171 H5N1 outbreaks in cattle, and in 2024, 917 herds were infected.
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部正在积极跟踪和确认牛和家禽中的 H5N1 暴发。没有提供有关具体措施或风险评估的进一步细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

USDA confirms new H5N1 avian flu cases in poultry, dairy cattle

News Brief
The US Department of Agriculture (USDA) confirmed new H5N1 avian flu cases in both commercial poultry and dairy cattle, with recent updates reported on August 27, 2026. In South Dakota, a large outbreak affected 29,700 birds in Edmunds County, and another 2,800 birds were impacted in Brule County. Additionally, three dairy cow herds in Texas and Utah were confirmed to have H5N1 infections. This year, 85 cattle herds have been infected with H5N1, compared to 171 in the same period last year and 917 in 2024. The USDA is actively monitoring these outbreaks, but no further details on specific response measures or risk assessments were provided. The outbreaks highlight the ongoing spread of H5N1 among livestock, raising concerns about potential impacts on animal health and food safety.
