Australia: SA Reports 1st H5N1 Detection in a Marine Mammal (long-nosed fur seal) - Avian Flu Diary

Australia: SA Reports 1st H5N1 Detection in a Marine Mammal (long-nosed fur seal) - Avian Flu Diary
新闻简报挪威兽医研究所报告,2026 年 8 月 25 日在挪威斯瓦尔巴群岛的一只北极熊身上检测到 HPAI H5N5。这是继 2026 年 5 月检测到类似病毒后,挪威和欧洲第二例确诊的北极熊感染 HPAI H5N5 病毒。HPAI H5N5 病毒已在斯瓦尔巴群岛的多种野生动物物种中发现,包括 野生鸟类、海象和北极狐,存在潜在暴露风险对人类。2026 年国家风险登记册已扩展至 223 页,将大流行性流感和 X 病列为首要威胁,强调公众做好准备的必要性。报告指出,虽然大多数北极熊在 H5N5 感染后幸存下来,但也可能会出现严重的疾病。该研究所正在进行测试和调查,但没有详细说明具体的遏制措施。该事件是在有关应急准备和 禽流感 病毒带来的风险的更广泛讨论的背景下报道的。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告发布于2026年8月23日,事件日期为2026年8月25日。报告的时间范围包括2026年国家风险登记册更新前后的时间段。
地点与对象
该事件发生在挪威斯瓦尔巴群岛,涉及北极熊和其他野生动物。受影响的种群包括北极熊和北极动物,人类也有可能接触到它们。
事件
挪威兽医研究所报告称,在斯瓦尔巴群岛的一只北极熊身上检测到了 HPAI H5N5。这是继 2026 年 5 月发现的第二例北极熊 HPAI H5N5 确诊病例之后。
规模、影响与风险
HPAI H5N5 病毒已在斯瓦尔巴群岛的多个物种中检测到,包括 野生鸟类、海象和北极熊。该风险被强调为重大,《2026 年国家风险登记册》将大流行性流感和 X 病列为首要威胁。据报道,这种病毒在某些情况下会导致严重疾病,但大多数北极熊似乎都能在感染后幸存下来。
应对、状态与不确定性
挪威兽医研究所正在进行测试和调查。报告指出,已在北极熊和其他野生动物体内检测到该病毒,但没有提供有关遏制或应对措施的进一步细节。《2026 年国家风险登记册》强调了公众为潜在流行病和其他风险做好准备的重要性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Australia: SA Reports 1st H5N1 Detection in a Marine Mammal (long-nosed fur seal) - Avian Flu Diary

News Brief
The Norwegian Veterinary Institute reported the detection of HPAI H5N5 in a polar bear on Svalbard Island, Norway, on August 25, 2026. This marks the second confirmed case of the virus in polar bears in Norway and Europe, following a similar detection in May 2026. The HPAI H5N5 virus has been found in various wildlife species on Svalbard, including wild birds, walruses, and arctic foxes, with potential exposure to humans. The National Risk Register 2026, which has expanded to 223 pages, lists pandemic flu and Disease X as top threats, emphasizing the need for public preparedness. The report notes that while most polar bears survive H5N5 infection, severe illness can occur. The institute is conducting tests and investigations, but no specific containment measures are detailed. The event was reported in the context of broader discussions on emergency preparedness and the risks posed by avian influenza viruses.
Original source text (English)
Avian Flu Diary Covering Pandemic and Seasonal Flu, Emerging Infectious Diseases, public health, community & Individual preparedness. NOTE: All AFD blogs are written by a human - any mistakes are solely mine. Thursday, August 27, 2026 UK National Risk Register 2026 - Urges Citizen Preparedness #19,311 In August of 2008 we looked at the first UK National Risk Register - which ran just 52 pages - and cited pandemic flu as the #1 threat facing the nation. It's prep advice to the public was fairly limited, essentially urging: Creating a core stock of essential supplies. This could include bottled water, a battery powered radio, torch, tinned food and alternative heating sources. Four years later, a slightly larger (58 page) 2012 update was released, which kept pandemic flu at the top of their threat board, but added coastal flooding, terrorist attacks, severe space weather, prolonged power or gas outages, and even volcanic threats to the list. The last time we looked at the UK Risk Register was in August of 2023, which by that time has expanded to nearly 200 pages. That edition of the NRR detailed 89 risks, within 9 risk themes: • Terrorism • Cyber • State threats • Geographic and diplomatic • Accidents and systems failures • Natural and environmental hazards • Human, animal and plant health • Societal • Conflict and instability Since then the world has changed radically, with the rise of A.I. and cyber threats, increasing global tensions in Europe and the Middle East, climate change and a record setting El Nino on the horizon, aging infrastructures, and growing economic threats. The 2026 edition of the NRR has expanded to a daunting 223 pages - so large that it is accompanied by a 27-page `Guide to the 2026 Risk Register' - which will hopefully provide a more palatable gateway for casual reader. Admittedly, the NRR isn't geared to the general public, as explained below: This edition of the NRR is available to all but not targeted at the general public. Advice for individuals, households and communities on preparing for a wide range of risks can be found on the Prepare website. In recent years we've seen a growing willingness of European nations to ask citizens to prepare for emergencies, including 2025's Denk Vooruit: The Netherlands National Citizen Preparedness Drive and last year's EU Commission call for all households to prepare for a wide array of disasters. The NRR guide goes on to say: The risks outlined in the 2026 NRR are significant, but they are not inevitable. Resilience is a national endeavour that relies on a combination of robust government coordination and the proactive responsibility of every individual, business, and community. It is particularly important for owners and operators of CNI to ensure continued delivery of the essential services that underpin UK society and way of life.This “whole-of-society” approach is built on deep partnerships, where we recognise our vital roles in preparing for, responding to, and recovering from emergencies. The power of collective readiness When communities take steps to prepare, they act as a force multiplier for national resilience. By managing what they can at home and with those around them, those in a position to do so reduce the immediate demand on frontline services. This ensures that emergency responders and limited resources can be prioritised for those who may need more support or who are in the greatest need. This collective effort is vital to ensure that the national resilience system remains strong enough to protect everyone when it matters most.For further guidance, resources, and advice on bolstering personal and household readiness, visit the Prepare website. While the full 2026 NRR provides a remarkably detailed UK-centric disaster reference guide, it isn't exactly light reading. Most will find the Guide to the NRR - which still puts a `Disease X' pandemic at the top of their threats list - far more accessible. Since it is impossible to summary a 223 page document here, I'll simply provide the links and recommend you download, and peruse, both. Documents National Risk Register 2026 PDF, 22.6 MB, 223 pages A Guide to the National Risk Register 2026 PDF, 4.75 MB, 27 pages Next month is National Preparedness Month, and - as we've done every year for nearly 2 decades - AFD will focus a good deal on individual, community, and national preparedness. Having survived more than a few hurricanes, my personal recommendations include: A battery operated NWS Emergency Radio to find out what was going on, and to get vital instructions from emergency officials A decent first-aid kit, so that you can treat injuries Enough non-perishable food and water on hand to feed and hydrate your family (including pets) for the duration A way to provide light when the grid is down. A way to cook safely without electricity A way to purify or filter water A way to handle basic sanitation and waste disposal. A way to stay cool (fans) or warm when the power is out. A small supply of cash to use in case credit/debit machines are not working An emergency plan, including meeting places, emergency out-of-state contact numbers, a disaster buddy, and in case you must evacuate, a bug-out bag Spare supply of essential prescription medicines that you or your family may need A way to entertain yourself, or your kids, during a prolonged blackout Being prepared doesn't guarantee you and your loved ones will come through a major disaster unscathed. But it is relatively cheap insurance, and when things do go sideways, it can substantially improve your chances. Wednesday, August 26, 2026 Norwegian Veterinary Institute Reports Another HPAI H5N5 Infected Polar Bear on Svalbard Island Credit: Multiple transatlantic incursions of HPAI clade 2.3.4.4b A(H5N5) virus into North America and spillover to mammals #19,310 Although most of the world is primarily concerned with HPAI H5N1, in northern Europe, Iceland, and parts of Canada, a different avian flu subtype - HPAI H5N5 - has been making inroads as well. Additionally, here in the United States HPAI H5N5 infected and killed a resident of Washington State last November, following contact with backyard birds. In the summer of 2022, the Norwegian Veterinary Institute reported both H5N1 and H5N5 for the first time in wild birds on Svalbard, which lies above the Arctic circle (see More HPAI (H5N5 & H5N1) Detected In Arctic (Svalbard). Last May, in Norway Veterinary Institute Reports HPAI H5N5 In Polar Bears on Svalbard Island, we looked at a report of the recent detection of HPAI H5N5 virus in both a dead walrus and a polar bear on Svalbard Island. Although no other animals were confirmed infected during this investigation, there were eye witness accounts of two other polar bears exhibiting potential neurological symptoms. Five weeks ago, in Preprint: HPAI H5N5 in a Polar Bear and Atlantic Walrus, Svalbard, 2026, with Widespread Seroconversion in Polar Bears, we saw a report indicating that H5N5 exposure is far more common among polar bears on Svalbard island than previously thought. Somewhat surprisingly, most polar bears appear to survive H5N5 infection, although severe (and likely neurotropic) illness can occur. Yesterday the Norwegian Veterinary Institute reported on a second polar bear - recovered in June some 23 km distant from the one found in May - has tested positive for H5N5. (translation) Bird flu detected in another polar bear in Svalbard Published 25.08.2026 The Norwegian Veterinary Institute has detected avian influenza in a young polar bear found dead on the northern tip of Spitsbergen. This is the second time the virus has been detected in polar bears in Norway and Europe. The detected highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) virus is of the subtype H5N5, a type of the virus that has been previously detected in Svalbard: In wild birds for the first time in 2022 , in a walrus in 2023 , in arctic foxes in 2025 , and in a polar bear and walrus in the spring of 2026. This latest detection is the second in polar bears in Norway and Europe. Highly pathogenic avian influenza viruses have also previously been detected in carnivorous mammals such as red foxes, otters and lynxes on mainland Norway after infection from wild birds. Sampled in the field by the District Governor The polar bear, a young individual, was found dead on Æøya, near Virgohamna. The animal was sampled by the Governor in June 2026, after avian influenza virus was detected in a dead polar bear and a dead walrus in Raudfjorden in May 2026. The distance between the two findings of dead animals is approximately 23 kilometers. - After the findings on the polar bear and walrus in Raudfjorden, we also recommended sampling the polar bear that was on Æøya, says veterinarian and wildlife health specialist at the Veterinary Institute Knut Madslien. The samples from the polar bear were then sent to the Norwegian Veterinary Institute and tested for avian influenza virus and rabies. Highly pathogenic avian influenza virus was detected in samples from both the brain and nose. Rabies virus was not detected. Bird flu virus is circulating in the highlands "The findings are part of a trend where highly pathogenic avian influenza virus is increasingly being detected in mammals in Europe. At the same time, the virus has spread to new areas in recent years, including the Arctic, where it may have consequences for vulnerable populations and ecosystems," explains Ragnhild Tønnessen, avian influenza coordinator at the Norwegian Veterinary Institute. - Our researchers, in close collaboration with the Norwegian Polar Institute and other partners, have found that a large proportion of the polar bears examined in Svalbard after 2022 have been exposed to previous avian influenza infection. Although we currently have limited knowledge of the consequences this has for the polar bears, the results indicate that the virus is circulating in the population and that exposure is more widespread than detections in dead animals alone would indicate, says Tønnessen. Increased infection pressure in mammals Most avian influenza viruses are best adapted to be transmitted between birds. However, mammals can sometimes be infected through direct contact with sick or dead birds or other mammals infected with the virus. The Veterinary Institute will conduct further investigations of the detected virus to characterize it more closely and clarify whether it shows signs of being specifically adapted to mammals. Second detection in polar bears in Europe In other predators, such as red foxes and lynxes, avian influenza can cause clinical signs of brain disease. Neurological signs such as circling gait, tilted head position, paralysis and reduced shyness towards humans are common. Avian influenza virus in polar bears has been scientifically published twice previously: In a young male polar bear found dead in Alaska in August 2023 and in a young male polar bear found dead in Raudfjorden on Svalbard in May 2026. However, it is not known how the virus affects individual individuals or the population of polar bears. – This finding strengthens the indications that highly pathogenic avian influenza virus can also affect polar bears, and that young individuals are particularly vulnerable. This is also consistent with findings in other carnivorous mammals, where the virus mainly affects younger individuals. However, the knowledge base is still very limited and it is important to gain more knowledge about how the virus can affect polar bears, says Madslien. Report if you observe dead or sick animals and birds Both highly pathogenic avian influenza and rabies are serious diseases found in Svalbard and can be transmitted to humans. Contact with sick and dead animals should therefore be avoided. If sick or dead animals are observed on Svalbard, it is important that the findings are reported to the Governor . This is important to obtain an overview of disease incidence and mortality, although it does not always trigger sampling. If avian influenza is suspected in birds and other animals on the mainland, the Norwegian Food Safety Authority must be notified . The Norwegian Veterinary Institute is the national reference laboratory for avian influenza and has molecular methods for the detection and characterization of avian influenza viruses. Admittedly, HPAI H5N5 remains a minor player in the world of avian flu, but it has shown remarkable tenacity, persisting and even thriving in some high latitude regions of the Northern Hemisphere. A recent study (see J. Virology: Risk assessment of avian influenza A(H5N5) virus from the first human case using the ferret model) found that while the Washington State isolate replicated robustly in mammalian cells and caused severe disease, it still lacked adaptations supporting efficient airborne spread. Six years ago HPAI H5N8 subtype dominated the world's avian flyways, only to be abruptly supplanted by a new and improved H5N1 in 2021. A reminder of just how quickly the avian flu-scape can change, and why we continue to watch this subtype with interest. Australia H5N1 Update: CSIRO Testing 1st Land Mammal (Red Fox) For Suspected Infection #19,309 Our almost daily dose of increasingly bad H5N1 news from Australia continues with an announcement today that their first suspected HPAI infection in a land mammal (a red fox recovered in Osborne) - a suburb of Adelaide - has been forwarded to their national testing lab for confirmation. Their progression of the virus moving from migratory birds to local birds to marine mammals and now potentially to land mammals (and likely on to poultry and/or livestock in the future) mirrors what we've seen in other regions of the world following the arrival of HPAI. South Australia continues to be the nation's hotspot for HPAI detections, although only a small fraction of the more than 29,000 citizen hotline reports have been investigated across the nation. As we saw 2 weeks ago, in Australia Changes How They Report H5N1- Australia now only counts `events' - not individual confirmed infections. So one event could represent multiple bird/animal deaths. H5 bird flu testing update 26 August Wednesday 26 August 2026 BiosecurityMedia release There are five new suspect H5 bird flu events in South Australia since yesterday’s update. The new suspect events include: Australian raven Limestone Coast – Robe Little penguin Limestone Coast – Southend Greater crested tern Murray and Mallee – Coonalpyn Pacific gull Kangaroo Island – Baudin Beach Red fox Metropolitan – Osborne Samples from these events have been sent to ACDP for confirmatory testing, with results expected to be known in the coming days. This brings the current number of suspect H5 bird flu events in South Australia to 41. The CSIRO’s Australian Centre for Disease Preparedness (ACDP) has no new confirmed H5 bird flu events in South Australia since yesterday’s update. There are 195 confirmed H5 bird flu events in South Australia. In line with national protocols, the South Australian Government recently changed how we report on H5 bird flu detections. Reporting is now based on events, rather than individual animal detections, where an event may involve one or more animals. The number of positive events captures all previously reported individual confirmed detections. An interactive map is available on the H5 bird flu website with up-to-date information on event locations: see current situation There remains no H5 bird flu detections in poultry or other livestock. The risk to human health of H5 bird flu remains low. Engagement with primary industries, the wildlife sector, and the broader South Australian community continues as part of the South Australian Government’s commitment to deliver the most comprehensive and effective wildlife and industry surveillance, reporting and preparedness efforts possible. South Australians are encouraged to continue being vigilant in reporting sick and dead birds and other wildlife. Avoid touching sick or dead birds and other wildlife. Record what you see. Report it to the Emergency Animal Disease Hotline on 1800 675 888. For the latest updates, information and advice about H5 bird flu in South Australia, including how to register for upcoming community forums, including tomorrow night (27 August) at Port MacDonnell, visit birdflu.sa.gov.au. Yesterday South Australia also announced the approval of bird flu vaccinations for a limited number of endangered native birds residing at Cleland Wildlife Park has been approved. Bird flu vaccination program approved for Cleland Wildlife Park Native birds at Cleland Wildlife Park − including critically endangered species such as orange-bellied parrots and regent honeyeaters – have been approved for vaccination as part of the South Australian Government’s response to H5 bird flu. The vaccination program has also been approved for a range of other native bird species at Cleland Wildlife Park, including: Magpie geese Australian pelicans Regent parrots Red-tailed black cockatoos Developed by the South Australian Government, the vaccination program at Cleland will initially see up to 100 native birds vaccinated across over 30 different species. The program has received the relevant approvals in line with the national framework that supports targeted vaccination of priority native bird species that are captive or managed. A range of factors are considered when identifying priority species for vaccination, including the conservation status and value of the species, the likelihood of catching bird flu, and whether vaccination can be delivered safely and effectively, according to regulatory requirements. Alveo Technologies 与 AgriNerds 合作,为美国家禽养殖户创建首个动态 禽流感 监测 系统

Alveo Technologies Partners with AgriNerds to Create First-of-its-Kind, Dynamic Avian Influenza Surveillance System for U.S. Poultry Farmers
新闻简报Alveo Technologies 和 AgriNerds Inc. 宣布建立战略合作伙伴关系,为美国家禽养殖户开发动态 禽流感 监测 系统。该计划将 Alveo 的快速分子诊断测试与 AgriNerds 的水禽警报网络相结合,该网络使用卫星图像、天气雷达和遥测技术来预测候鸟活动并生成每日风险评分。该系统旨在通过在风险升高时提供有针对性的检测来实现 禽流感 的早期检测,从而减少病毒的传播并降低农民的成本。水禽警报网络已被 25 个州的 1,500 多个农场使用,此次合作的目的是通过测试数据的反馈循环来提高其准确性。这是确认的合作伙伴关系和开发工作,而不是 暴发。该系统旨在支持在家禽和畜牧业中采取主动、风险知情的疾病管理方法。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该合作伙伴关系于 2026 年 8 月 25 日宣布,该系统旨在提供每日风险评估和实时更新。
地点与对象
该系统主要针对美国家禽养殖户,特别是 25 个州的养殖户,旨在监测农场层面的家禽和畜牧业风险。
事件
Alveo Technologies 和 AgriNerds Inc. 宣布建立战略合作伙伴关系,共同开发动态 禽流感 监测 系统,该系统将快速分子测试与基于候鸟活动的实时风险建模相结合。这是已确认的合作伙伴关系和倡议,而不是 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
该系统旨在通过早期检测和有针对性的检测来减少高致病性禽流感的传播。它旨在降低成本并提高效率,并有可能随着时间的推移改进风险评估。该系统计划供 25 个州的 1,500 多个农场使用。
应对、状态与不确定性
合作伙伴关系正在进行中,正在开发的系统可提供实时风险评分和快速测试功能。该计划基于将 Alveo 的分子诊断与 AgriNerds 的水禽警报网络相结合,该网络使用卫星图像、气象雷达和遥测技术。该系统尚未投入运行,但正在实施以支持主动疾病管理。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Alveo Technologies Partners with AgriNerds to Create First-of-its-Kind, Dynamic Avian Influenza Surveillance System for U.S. Poultry Farmers

News Brief
Alveo Technologies and AgriNerds Inc. have announced a strategic partnership to develop a dynamic avian influenza surveillance system for U.S. poultry farmers. This initiative combines Alveo's rapid molecular diagnostic testing with AgriNerds' Waterfowl Alert Network, which uses satellite imagery, weather radar, and telemetry to predict migratory bird activity and generate daily risk scores. The system is designed to enable early detection of avian influenza by providing targeted testing when risk is elevated, thus reducing the spread of the virus and lowering costs for farmers. The Waterfowl Alert Network is already used by over 1,500 farms across 25 states, and the partnership aims to enhance its accuracy through a feedback loop of test data. This is a confirmed partnership and development effort, not an outbreak. The system is intended to support a proactive, risk-informed approach to disease management in the poultry and livestock industry.
Original source text (English)

Partners with AgriNerds to Create First-of-its-Kind, Dynamic Avian Influenza Surveillance System for U.S. Poultry Farmers [August 25, 2026] Alveo Technologies Partners with AgriNerds to Create First-of-its-Kind, Dynamic Avian Influenza Surveillance System for U.S. Poultry Farmers Tweet Linking on-site, rapid molecular HPAI testing to real-time HPAI modeling of migratory birds to help farmers run dynamic, risk-informed surveillance programs ALAMEDA, Calif., Aug. 25, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Alveo Technologies, Inc., a global leader in rapid, point-of-need molecular diagnostics for early disease detection, today announced a strategic partnership with AgriNerds Inc, which has developed the Waterfowl Alert Network, a first-of-its-kind dynamic surveillance and awareness program that predicts historic and daily waterfowl activity around the nation's poultry and livestock farms. The partnership will pair Alveo's unique, rapid, molecular diagnostic with AgriNerds' Waterfowl Alert Network, a proprietary system that uses a combination of satellite imagery, weather radar, and telemetry to create daily farm-level HPAI risk scores based on remote sensing and HPAI surveillance data. To defend against HPAI and other pathogens, poultry producers have heavily invested in strengthening their biosecurity programs, but farms continue to suffer from HPAI outbreaks triggered by migratory birds, which is the source of infection for 68% of US H5N1 outbreaks since 2022, according to the APHIS. Currenty, producers typically take one of two strategies for testing. Most test only when animals display flu-like symptoms, but this approach risks detecting the virus too late to prevent it from spreading. Some conduct periodic blind surveillance testing, which has the advantage of enabling early detection, but can be expensive. Alveo and AgriNerds are partnering to develop a new rapid and targeted approach: dynamic surveillance that alerts farmers when the risk of infection is high. AgriNerds' Waterfowl Alert Network will use radar imagery to detect migratory bird movements, analyze the data using AI-powered pattern recognition software, and generate daily risk scores it shares with customers. When risk escalates, Alveo's on-farm molecular test will help farms to conduct asymptomatic surveillance testing that provides precise results in under an hour, as opposed to waiting days for traditional PCR results to arrive from a lab. This targeted, risk-based testing approach is designed to lower costs and increase effectiveness, so farmers can detect the virus early and reduce its spread. "To stop the spread of HPAI, producers need tools that will enable them to monitor risk and, when it's elevated, employ rapid, accurate tests so they can take quick action and prevent further infection," said Shaun Holt, CEO of Alveo Technologies "This dynamic solution will aggregate and simplify rich data from AgriNerds' and deliver sophisticated risk assessments to users phones in a simple red, yellow and green format. They can then run surveillance tests based on informed risk and rapidly respond if bird flu is detected." The two organizations will use this feedback loop to strengthen the accuracy of risk assessments over time. As positive and negative test data accumulate across regions and seasons, both the risk alerts and testing guidance will become increasingly precise and valuable, securing the nation's food supply as well as producers' businesses. "Biosecurity extends far beyond the facility's fence line," said Maurice Pitesky, professor at UC Davis and CEO and co-founder of AgriNerds. "Our Waterfowl Alert Network gives farmers visibility into the dynamic risk landscape around their operations. When paired with Alveo's testing capability and rapid results, farmers can respond to real risks in real time. In this way, agriculture can move past reactive disease management to adopt a proactive, risk-informed approach." About AgriNerds: AgriNerds' veterinarians, epidemiologists, engineers, and waterfowl biologists are leveraging and repurposing various remote sensing-based technologies and applying modern statistical methods to help farmers usher in practical "off-site" risk assessments and HPAI risk predictions. The WaterFowl Alert Network is used by over 1,500 farms across 25 states and has the capacity to make historic and daily risk predictions for the entire U.S. poultry and livestock industry at the farm level. For more information, visit www.agrinerds.com. About Alveo Technologies: Alveo Technologies is a molecular diagnostics company that has developed a portable, cloud-connected platform that brings laboratory-quality pathogen detection directly to the Point of NeedTM for farmers, veterinarians and public health officials. By decentralizing molecular diagnostics, Alveo enables rapid detection and decision-making to help protect animal and human health, food security and supply chain resiliency. Alveo Sense Poultry provides avian influenza detection in approximately one hour, with results delivered through the Alveo Sense app. The Alveo platform incorporates machine learning technology to analyze assay data and provide automated interpretation of test results. Its growing portfolio includes molecular tests for avian influenza and other high-consequence pathogens. To learn more, visit alveotechnologies.com or follow us on LinkedIn. Product Availability Disclaimer: Product availability may vary by country or jurisdiction and is subject to applicable regulatory requirements and approvals. For information regarding availability in your country or jurisdiction, please contact Alveo Technologies at [email protected]. Media contact Shermineh Rohanizadeh / Colleen Irish Mindset Agency for Alveo Technologies [email protected] View original content to download multimedia:https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/alveo-technologies-partners-with-agrinerds-to-create-first-of-its-kind-dynamic-avian-influenza-surveillance-system-for-us-poultry-farmers-302859048.html SOURCE Alveo Technologies [ Back To TMCnet.com's Homepage ]

澳洲确认首起本土哺乳动物感染H5N1禽流感病例 - 联合早报

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报澳大利亚确认了首例本土哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感 病毒的病例,该病毒是在南澳大利亚的一只长鼻海狗身上发现的。这只海狗被发现死在阿德莱德以南约 310 公里的海滨小镇海滩港,没有受伤的迹象。这标志着该国首例已知的哺乳动物感染 H5N1 病毒。澳大利亚政府指出,这种感染似乎与其他海洋哺乳动物的死亡无关。H5N1 病毒于 6 月在澳大利亚首次发现,此后在 野生鸟类 中传播。当局正在积极监测局势并采取预防措施,包括为菲利普岛的野生企鹅接种疫苗,以遏制病毒的传播。该病例凸显了 禽流感 蔓延至哺乳动物 宿主 的可能性,引发了对人畜共患 传播 的担忧以及持续保持警惕的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
澳大利亚南澳大利亚。
事件
澳大利亚确认首例本土哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感 病毒的病例,该病毒是在长鼻海狗身上发现的。
规模、影响与风险
自6月以来,H5N1病毒一直在澳大利亚的野生鸟类中传播,海狗的感染表明跨物种溢出对哺乳动物有潜在的影响。澳大利亚政府正在监测疫情的传播并采取预防措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚政府正在调查此案,并表示海狗的死亡似乎与其他海洋哺乳动物无关。目前情况正在受到积极监测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳洲确认首起本土哺乳动物感染H5N1禽流感病例 - 联合早报
News Brief
Australia has confirmed the first case of a native mammal infected with H5N1 avian influenza virus, found in a long-nosed fur seal in South Australia. The fur seal was discovered dead in the coastal town of Beachport, approximately 310 kilometers south of Adelaide, with no signs of injury. This marks the first known instance of a mammal contracting H5N1 in the country. The Australian government has noted that the infection does not seem to be connected to other marine mammals' deaths. H5N1 was first detected in Australia in June and has since spread among wild birds. Authorities are actively monitoring the situation and have taken preventive measures, including vaccinating wild penguins on Phillip Island to curb the spread of the virus. The case highlights the potential for avian influenza to spill over into mammalian hosts, raising concerns about zoonotic transmission and the need for continued vigilance.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

另一只海豹确诊后,赶紧为小企鹅接种 H5N1 疫苗 - SBS

Rush to vaccinate little penguins against H5N1 as another seal is diagnosed - SBS
新闻简报2026 年 8 月,澳大利亚报告南澳大利亚第二只海豹感染 H5N1 禽流感,促使菲利普岛上的小企鹅开展 疫苗接种 活动。周日,在海滩港的一只长鼻海狗身上确认了第一例哺乳动物感染该病毒的病例,据信是从 野生鸟类 感染的。科学家们正在为 5,000 只小企鹅群体接种疫苗,其中约 1,000 只已经接种了第一剂疫苗。企鹅可预测的夜间行为使它们成为 疫苗接种 的理想候选者。环境部长默里·瓦特表示,从鸟类到哺乳动物的传播是不可避免的,政府正在考虑进一步的 疫苗接种 计划。农业部长朱莉·柯林斯(Julie Collins)提到,北美正在进行针对哺乳动物的禽流感疫苗试验,但尚未商业化。疫苗接种 流程需要进行实验和微调,团队工作到深夜才能有效管理该项目。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 8 月 24 日,周日确诊第一例病例,周一诊断出第二例海豹病例。
地点与对象
南澳大利亚州和维多利亚州,特别是菲利普岛,那里有 5,000 只小企鹅正在接受疫苗接种。
事件
南澳大利亚第二只海豹感染了 H5N1 禽流感,促使当局为菲利普岛的小企鹅接种疫苗以预防该病毒。
规模、影响与风险
南澳大利亚记录了 5 例疑似 H5N1 病例,其中包括一只长鼻海豹。企鹅 疫苗接种 计划已为约 1,000 人接种了疫苗,并计划继续下去。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局正在实施 疫苗接种 计划,测试海豹,并调查禽流感 疫苗 对哺乳动物的可用性。该病毒向哺乳动物的传播被认为是不可避免的,政府正在研究进一步的措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rush to vaccinate little penguins against H5N1 as another seal is diagnosed - SBS

News Brief
In August 2026, Australia reported a second seal infected with H5N1 bird flu in South Australia, prompting a vaccination drive for little penguins on Phillip Island. The first case of the virus in a mammal was confirmed on Sunday in a long-nosed fur seal at Beachport, believed to have contracted it from wild birds. Scientists are vaccinating a 5,000-strong colony of little penguins, with about 1,000 already receiving their first dose. The penguins' predictable nightly behavior made them ideal candidates for vaccination. Environment Minister Murray Watt stated that the spread from birds to mammals is inevitable, and the government is considering further vaccination programs. Agriculture Minister Julie Collins mentioned that trials for bird flu vaccines in mammals are ongoing in North America, but nothing is commercially available yet. The vaccination process has required experimentation and fine-tuning, with teams working late into the night to manage the program efficiently.
Original source text (English)
Australia Rush to vaccinate little penguins against H5N1 as another seal is diagnosed A second seal has contracted the virus in South Australia, as Phillip Island rushes to vaccinate its iconic bird population. Scientists hope to vaccinate the 5,000-strong colony of little penguins on Phillip Island. Source: AAP / Joel Carrett 3 min read Published 24 August 2026 11:22am Source : AAP Skip to article content In brief Five suspected H5 bird flu cases were recorded in South Australia on Monday, including a long-nosed fur seal. 5,000 little penguins in Victoria are receiving a vaccine to protect them against the deadly virus. Bird flu has likely spread to a second seal, one day after the first mammal in Australia was confirmed to have contracted the virus. Five suspected H5 bird flu cases were recorded in South Australia on Monday, including a long-nosed fur seal at The Pages Conservation Park, the state's Department of Primary Industries and Regions said in a statement on Monday morning. The Pages — which covers two small islands between Kangaroo Island and the mainland — is a haven for the endangered Australian sea lion. The fur seal has been tested for bird flu, with results expected in the coming days. The first positive case of the virus in a mammal was confirmed on Sunday in a long-nosed fur seal at Beachport, 300km south of Adelaide, which authorities believed was contracted from wild birds. News that makes sense Your trusted source for staying up-to-date with the world around you. Get free daily news updates and analysis, straight to your inbox. Before the new case was announced, Environment Minister Murray Watt said the spread from birds to mammals was inevitable and the government was examining whether further vaccination programs were needed. "We are likely to see mass mortalities of birds and mammals, and I know that is a very distressing thing for Australians to hear. But we also need to be realistic," he told ABC TV on Monday. "This is what we've seen happen overseas. Australia is very fortunate that we've been the last continent on Earth to have this strain of bird flu happen. But we are likely to see those kind of events as we have overseas." Agriculture Minister Julie Collins said the federal government was investigating whether bird flu vaccines were available for use in mammals. "There are trials underway in North America, in California and Hawaii, and this is using a vaccine that's been developed for cattle," she told ABC radio. "There certainly is nothing commercially available, but we do continue to look at that research." Bird flu vaccinations have been rolled out to at-risk species, including the little penguins on Victoria's Phillip Island. Many were corralled into pens to receive the first of two vaccinations on Sunday night. With case numbers on the rise, authorities have stepped in to do what they can to defend the 5,000-strong colony. The penguins' nightly ritual of returning to the same burrow, along the same path, made them ideal candidates for vaccination, Phillip Island Nature Parks reserve manager Ben Thomas said. As a world-first in scale, the process has taken some experimentation and fine-tuning. "We've had our teams out late into the night for a week now and learning a lot along the process," Thomas said. "We started on some of the quieter penguin pathways to fine-tune the whole process and get it working as quickly as we can, so we can process as many penguins as we can each night. "We've now moved to the western end of the penguin parade, where the larger numbers are, and it's working really well." A further 600 penguins were vaccinated over the weekend, taking the total number that have received a first dose at Phillip Island and St Kilda to about 1,000. For the latest from SBS News, download our app and subscribe to our newsletter

Scale, impact and risk
Five suspected H5N1 cases were recorded in South Australia, including a long-nosed fur seal. The penguin vaccination program has vaccinated about 1,000 individuals, with plans to continue.
Response, status and uncertainty
Authorities are conducting vaccination programs, testing seals, and investigating the availability of bird flu vaccines for mammals. The spread of the virus to mammals is considered inevitable, and the government is examining further measures.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

全球最后"净土"告破!西澳首现H5N1高致病性禽流感,赫德岛已致逾1.3万头海豹幼崽惨死 - 华人头条

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报The news report highlights the first confirmed case of H5N1 highly pathogenic avian influenza in Western Australia, detected in a wild brown skua near Cape Le Grand National Park. This marks the end of Australia's status as the last continent free from H5-type avian influenza. The virus, which is genetically linked to strains found on Heard Island and McDonald Island, has caused a devastating ecological impact, with over 13,000 seal pups dying on Heard Island, where mortality rates reached 76%-97% in some areas. The outbreak was traced back to a potential transmission from the French Crozet Islands in August 2025 via migratory birds. While no infections have been found in domestic poultry farms, the Australian government has activated a high-level monitoring system for susceptible bird species. Experts emphasize that the risk to the general public remains low, as there is no evidence of sustained human-to-human transmission. However, they advise caution, especially for those in contact with wildlife, and recommend reporting any sick or dead animals and maintaining hygiene. The government has allocated over 1.13 billion Australian dollars to address the outbreak, focusing on scientific response rather than panic.

全球最后"净土"告破!西澳首现H5N1高致病性禽流感,赫德岛已致逾1.3万头海豹幼崽惨死 - 华人头条
News Brief
The news report highlights the first confirmed case of H5N1 highly pathogenic avian influenza in Western Australia, detected in a wild brown skua near Cape Le Grand National Park. This marks the end of Australia's status as the last continent free from H5-type avian influenza. The virus, which is genetically linked to strains found on Heard Island and McDonald Island, has caused a devastating ecological impact, with over 13,000 seal pups dying on Heard Island, where mortality rates reached 76%-97% in some areas. The outbreak was traced back to a potential transmission from the French Crozet Islands in August 2025 via migratory birds. While no infections have been found in domestic poultry farms, the Australian government has activated a high-level monitoring system for susceptible bird species. Experts emphasize that the risk to the general public remains low, as there is no evidence of sustained human-to-human transmission. However, they advise caution, especially for those in contact with wildlife, and recommend reporting any sick or dead animals and maintaining hygiene. The government has allocated over 1.13 billion Australian dollars to address the outbreak, focusing on scientific response rather than panic.

新西兰新闻 华人号 2026-06-20 13:06 阅读量:6.6万+ 华人号:新西兰新闻 (本报综合报道)​ 当地时间2026年6月20日,澳大利亚联邦农业、渔业和林业部长朱莉·柯林斯(Julie Collins)正式确认:西澳大利亚州埃斯佩兰斯(Esperance)附近Cape Le Grand国家公园发现的一只野生棕贼鸥(brown skua),经CSIRO检测确诊感染H5N1型高致病性禽流感病毒,同一区域另一只巨鹱鸟初筛亦呈阳性。这意味着,澳大利亚——这个曾被视为全球唯一未受H5型禽流感侵扰的大陆,正式"失守"。 候鸟携毒登陆,西澳偏远海岸现死鸟 据西澳农业部披露,6月14日,珀斯东南约700公里的埃斯佩兰斯以东56公里处海滩,志愿者发现一只行为异常、无法飞行的迁徙海鸟(棕贼鸥),该鸟于当晚死亡,样本连夜送检。6月20日上午,澳大利亚疾病预防中心出具最终报告:确认为H5N1高致病性禽流感阳性,与先前在澳海外领地赫德岛(Heard Island)检出的毒株同源。 联邦首席兽医官贝丝·库克森(Beth Cookson)表示,目前未在家禽养殖场发现感染,也未监测到本土野生动物大规模死亡,但全澳已启动最高级别野生鸟类监测网络,重点盯防黑天鹅、企鹅等易感物种。 南极前哨已沦陷:赫德岛生态惨剧 比本土首例更触目惊心的是,就在病例公布前一天(6月19日),澳大利亚南极项目(AAP)发布权威研究声明:高致病性H5N1禽流感已肆虐澳属亚南极领地——赫德岛及麦克唐纳岛。 无人机航测数据显示: 南象海豹幼崽平均死亡率达76%,部分聚居地高达97%,预估死亡数超13,000头; 王企鹅、巴布亚企鹅及南极毛皮海狮均检出病毒阳性,出现异常死亡; 基因溯源显示病毒可能于2025年8月自法属克罗泽特群岛经野生动物迁徙东传约1800公里抵岛。 生态学家警告,若病毒借候鸟进一步向澳本土湿地、沿海繁殖地扩散,澳洲独有的无尾熊(考拉除外,此处指特有水禽)及海洋哺乳动物将面临"最可怕的噩梦"。 家禽业严阵以待,暂未影响供应链 西澳农业部长杰基·贾维斯(Jackie Jarvis)称,发现点距商业家禽养殖区较远,全澳蛋鸡及肉鸡场暂未检出异常。政府已要求养殖户强化生物安全——必要时下达"圈养令"禁止散养、封堵野鸟接触饮水饲料,防止重演去年H7疫情导致200余万只蛋鸡被扑杀、超市鸡蛋断供的困局。 普通人要恐慌吗?专家:无需,但需警惕 澳洲卫生及公共卫生专家强调:目前无证据表明该毒株具备持续人际传播能力,普通人群感染风险低。人类感染多发生于密切接触染病/死亡动物的情况。 昆士兰大学传染病专家保罗·格里芬(Paul Griffin)教授提醒民众做到三点: 不触碰:发现生病、受伤或死亡的野鸟、海豹等野生动物,严禁徒手接触,保持距离拍照记录位置; 速上报:立即通过各州动物疫病热线(如澳洲1800 675 888)或birdflu.gov.au网站报告; 勤洗手+打疫苗:接触自然环境后认真洗手,季节性流感疫苗按时接种以保护基础免疫力。 澳总理阿尔巴尼斯表示,联邦已投入逾1.13亿澳元建立H5禽流感应对体系,"我们一直为此刻做准备,关键在于科学应对而非盲目焦虑"。 (本报将持续追踪CSIRO对第二只巨鹱鸟的最终分型结果及野生种群监测动态。) 澳联社及《自然》子刊预印本研究报道|2026年6月20日 澳大利亚评估人类感染H5N1风险为低 - UA.NEWS

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报澳大利亚疾病控制中心评估人类感染H5N1的风险为低,没有人类感染H5N1病例的记录。该中心表示,虽然已在海豹中检测到该病毒,但人传人的 传播 极为罕见。在全球范围内,自六年前 H5N1 2.3.4.4b 毒株在欧洲出现以来,已有 141 例人类感染和 24 例死亡,主要与与受感染动物的密切接触有关。大多数病例发生在家禽养殖场和奶牛场工人中。该病毒可以通过直接接触受感染的鸟类、其粪便、呼吸道分泌物或受污染的灰尘来传播。感染后一到十天可能出现的症状包括发烧、咳嗽、喉咙痛、流鼻涕、肌肉疼痛、头痛、疲劳、恶心和呕吐。该中心建议避免接触患病或死亡的鸟类,处理鸟类后洗手,彻底煮熟肉和蛋以降低风险。该报告是部分联合摘要,无法访问完整的原始文章。

地点与对象
所提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告地点或受影响的人口。
事件
澳大利亚疾病控制中心评估人类感染H5N1的风险为低,没有人类感染H5N1病例的记录。
规模、影响与风险
自六年前H5N1 2.3.4.4b毒株在欧洲出现以来,全球已有141人感染,24人死亡。大多数病例与与受感染动物的密切接触有关,特别是在家禽养殖场和奶牛场。传播 从动物传染给人类的风险非常罕见,而人传人的 传播 则极为罕见。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚疾病控制中心正在监测 突变,它可能使 H5N1 更适应哺乳动物。他们建议避免接触生病或死亡的鸟类,在处理鸟类之前和之后洗手,并确保彻底煮熟肉和蛋。来源是部分报告,无法提取完整的原始文章。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳大利亚评估人类感染H5N1风险为低 - UA.NEWS
News Brief
Australia's disease control center has assessed the risk of human infection with H5N1 as low, with no recorded cases of human H5N1 infection. The center stated that while the virus has been detected in seals, human-to-human transmission is extremely rare. Globally, since the H5N1 2.3.4.4b strain emerged in Europe six years ago, there have been 141 human infections and 24 deaths, primarily linked to close contact with infected animals. Most cases occur in poultry farm and dairy workers. The virus can spread through direct contact with infected birds, their feces, respiratory secretions, or contaminated dust. Symptoms, which may appear one to ten days after infection, include fever, cough, sore throat, runny nose, muscle pain, headache, fatigue, nausea, and vomiting. The center advises avoiding contact with sick or dead birds, washing hands after handling birds, and thoroughly cooking meat and eggs to reduce risk. The report is a partial syndicated summary, and the full original article could not be accessed.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

长鼻海狗验出H5N1 澳洲首起哺乳动物感染禽流感病例 - 早晨报

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报在南澳大利亚海滩港附近发现一只长鼻海豹死亡后,澳大利亚确认了首例 H5N1 禽流感 哺乳动物感染。州当局于 2026 年 8 月 23 日宣布了此案。联邦农业部长警告说,尽管尚未在家禽或农业环境中发现检测到的菌株,但 H5 禽流感 在环境中的广泛传播可能会造成重大损失。这只海豹没有明显的受伤迹象,它的死亡似乎与其他海洋哺乳动物的死亡无关。自 6 月以来,澳大利亚一直在应对 野生鸟类 中 H5N1 病例激增的情况,截至 8 月 21 日,全国确诊病例为 297 例。当局继续监测和测试,同时调查 传播 通往哺乳动物的潜在途径。
查看新闻五要素
时间
事件日期:提供的证据中未报告。发布日期:2026 年 8 月 23 日 14:31(澳大利亚东部时间)。
地点与对象
South Australia, Australia; coastal town of Beachport, approximately 310 km south of Adelaide; wild long-nosed fur seal population of about 100,000.
事件
澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感,死者是在南澳大利亚海滩港附近发现的一只长鼻海狗。南澳大利亚当局于 2026 年 8 月 23 日报告了这一发现。
规模、影响与风险
野生长鼻海狗出现一例确诊病例;澳大利亚没有人类、家禽或农业感染的报告。该病毒此前自2026年6月以来已在澳大利亚造成大规模野生鸟类死亡,截至2026年8月21日全国确诊病例297例。联邦官员警告说,H5 野生鸟类在环境中的广泛传播可能会导致重大损失。
应对、状态与不确定性
南澳大利亚当局于 2026 年 8 月 23 日确认了此案;联邦农业部长表示,这起死亡事件似乎与其他患病或死亡的海洋哺乳动物无关。国家测试和监测继续进行;没有证据表明澳大利亚家禽或农业中存在 H5N1 病毒。调查正在进行中;传播 通往哺乳动物的途径和潜在的更广泛的生态影响仍然存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
长鼻海狗验出H5N1 澳洲首起哺乳动物感染禽流感病例 - 早晨报
News Brief
Australia has confirmed its first mammal infection with H5N1 avian influenza after a long-nosed fur seal was found dead near Beachport in South Australia. The case was announced by state authorities on 23 August 2026. The federal agriculture minister warned that widespread circulation of H5 avian influenza in the environment could cause significant losses, though the detected strain has not yet been found in poultry or agricultural settings. The seal showed no visible injuries and its death appears unrelated to other marine mammal mortalities. Australia has been battling a surge in H5N1 cases among wild birds since June, with 297 confirmed cases nationwide as of 21 August. Authorities continue monitoring and testing while investigating potential transmission routes to mammals.

View five news elements
Time
Event date: Not reported in the supplied evidence. Publication date: 23 August 2026, 14:31 (AEST).
Location and population
South Australia, Australia; coastal town of Beachport, approximately 310 km south of Adelaide; wild long-nosed fur seal population of about 100,000.
Event
Australia confirmed its first mammal infection with H5N1 avian influenza in a long-nosed fur seal found dead near Beachport, South Australia. The detection was reported by South Australian authorities on 23 August 2026.
Scale, impact and risk
Single confirmed case in a wild long-nosed fur seal; no reported human, poultry, or agricultural infections in Australia. The virus has previously caused large-scale wild bird deaths in Australia since June 2026, with 297 confirmed cases nationwide as of 21 August 2026. Federal officials warn that widespread environmental circulation of H5 avian influenza could lead to significant losses.
Response, status and uncertainty
South Australian authorities confirmed the case on 23 August 2026; federal Agriculture Minister stated the death appears unrelated to other sick or dead marine mammals. National testing and surveillance continue; no evidence of H5N1 in Australian poultry or agriculture reported. Investigation ongoing; uncertainty remains regarding transmission pathways to mammals and potential broader ecological impact.

澳首次在哺乳动物身上 检测到H5N1禽流感病毒 - 8world.com

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报澳大利亚确认了首例在哺乳动物身上发现的 H5N1 禽流感 病毒病例,特别是在阿德莱德以南沿海小镇发现的一只长鼻海豹的尸体。海豹没有受伤的迹象,怀疑是通过接触受感染的 野生鸟类 或其环境而感染的。这标志着H5N1在野生鸟类中持续传播的重大进展,全国已有297例确诊病例。澳大利亚政府表示,尚未在家禽或农业部门检测到该病毒,对人类的风险仍然很低。8world.com 于 2026 年 8 月 23 日报道了该事件,消息来源被认为是可信的。调查仍在进行中,没有关于传播或对其他哺乳动物影响的进一步细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
澳大利亚,特别是阿德莱德以南的一个沿海小镇,有一只长鼻海狗。
事件
澳大利亚确认首例在哺乳动物中发现的 H5N1 禽流感 病毒病例。这只受感染的长鼻海狗被发现已经死亡,没有受伤的迹象。
规模、影响与风险
这是澳大利亚首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 病毒的确诊病例。该病毒已在野生鸟类中传播,全国确诊病例297例。由于在家禽或农业部门尚未检测到该病毒,因此对人类的风险被认为很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚政府正在监测局势,并表示目前家禽中不存在H5N1病毒。海豹感染的原因疑似与受感染的野生鸟类或其环境接触。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳首次在哺乳动物身上 检测到H5N1禽流感病毒 - 8world.com
News Brief
Australia has confirmed the first case of H5N1 avian influenza virus detected in a mammal, specifically a long-nosed fur seal found dead in a coastal town south of Adelaide. The seal showed no signs of injury, and the infection is suspected to have occurred through contact with infected wild birds or their environment. This marks a significant development in the ongoing spread of H5N1 among wild birds, with 297 confirmed cases nationwide. The Australian government has stated that the virus has not been detected in poultry or agricultural sectors, and the risk to humans remains low. The event was reported by 8world.com on 23 August 2026, and the source is considered reputable. The investigation is ongoing, with no further details on the spread or impact on other mammals.
Original source text (Chinese)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳洲首例哺乳动物患禽流感 海豹遗体检测出H5N1病毒 - Shangbao Indonesia

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 19 日,南澳大利亚海滩港一具长鼻海狗尸体检测呈阳性,澳大利亚确认首例 H5N1 禽流感 哺乳动物死亡。联邦农业部长表示,感染可能是由于接触受感染的 野生鸟类 所致。在全国范围内,已记录了 298 起 H5 事件,其中包括袋鼠岛 1,000 多只大凤头燕鸥和一只企鹅呈阳性的大规模死亡。当局警告说,濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮和海狗面临很高的感染风险,而人与人之间的传播仍然很低。联邦和州官员正在评估针对哺乳动物的实验性美国 疫苗,并敦促公众报告患病或死亡的鸟类,因为尚未在家禽中检测到该病毒。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-23;事件发生于 2026 年 8 月 19 日。
地点与对象
澳大利亚、南澳大利亚、海滩港(石灰岩海岸)和袋鼠岛;海洋哺乳动物(长鼻海狗)、海鸟(大凤头燕鸥)和企鹅。
事件
澳大利亚首次确认 H5N1 禽流感 导致哺乳动物死亡;澳大利亚农业部长 Julie Collins 和南澳大利亚州州长 Peter Malinauskas 报道;事件已确认。
规模、影响与风险
1 只确认哺乳动物死亡(长鼻海狗);全国共 298 场 H5 禽流感 活动(南澳大利亚州 183 场、维多利亚州 86 场、新南威尔士州 11 场、西澳大利亚州 10 场、塔斯马尼亚州 7 场、昆士兰州 1 场);近海岛屿超过 1,000 只凤头燕鸥死亡;1只企鹅检测呈阳性;对濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮和长鼻海狗构成高风险;人与人之间的 传播 风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
Federal and state governments assessing experimental US-origin vaccine for Australian mammals; authorities urge public to avoid sick or dead birds and report sightings to birdflu.gov.au; South Australia collaborating with federal government on vaccine assessment; Australian Marine Conservation Society calls for 200 million AUD wildlife recovery fund; H5 avian influenza has not yet been detected in domestic poultry or agriculture.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳洲首例哺乳动物患禽流感 海豹遗体检测出H5N1病毒 - Shangbao Indonesia
News Brief
Australia confirmed its first mammalian death from H5N1 avian influenza after a long-nosed fur seal carcass tested positive in South Australia’s Beachport on 19 August 2026. The federal agriculture minister stated the infection likely resulted from contact with infected wild birds. Nationwide, 298 H5 events have been recorded, including mass deaths of more than 1,000 greater crested terns and a single positive penguin on Kangaroo Island. Authorities warn that endangered Australian sea lions and fur seals face high infection risk, while human-to-human spread remains low. Federal and state officials are evaluating an experimental US vaccine for mammals and urge the public to report sick or dead birds, as the virus has not yet been detected in domestic poultry.
Original source text (Chinese)

View five news elements
Time
Published 2026-08-23; event occurred 2026-08-19.
Location and population
Australia, South Australia, Beachport (Limestone Coast), and Kangaroo Island; marine mammals (long-nosed fur seal), seabirds (greater crested terns), and penguins.
Event
First confirmed mammalian fatality from H5N1 avian influenza in Australia; reported by Australian Agriculture Minister Julie Collins and South Australia Premier Peter Malinauskas; event is confirmed.
Scale, impact and risk
1 confirmed mammalian death (long-nosed fur seal); 298 total H5 avian influenza events nationwide (183 in South Australia, 86 in Victoria, 11 in New South Wales, 10 in Western Australia, 7 in Tasmania, 1 in Queensland); 1,000+ greater crested tern deaths in offshore islands; 1 penguin tested positive; high risk to endangered Australian sea lions and long-nosed fur seals; human-to-human transmission risk remains low.
Response, status and uncertainty
Federal and state governments assessing experimental US-origin vaccine for Australian mammals; authorities urge public to avoid sick or dead birds and report sightings to birdflu.gov.au; South Australia collaborating with federal government on vaccine assessment; Australian Marine Conservation Society calls for 200 million AUD wildlife recovery fund; H5 avian influenza has not yet been detected in domestic poultry or agriculture.
原文 / Source

澳洲確認首例哺乳動物感染H5N1禽流感 - 大纪元

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报澳大利亚确认首例感染 H5N1 禽流感 的哺乳动物病例,其中一只长鼻海狗在南澳大利亚海滩港被发现死亡。该病毒已从 野生鸟类 传播到本地鸟类,但尚未在家禽养殖场或农业系统中检测到。澳大利亚政府正在密切关注事态发展,并指出海豹的死亡与其他海洋哺乳动物无关。由于靠近 野生鸟类,海豹感染 H5N1 的风险很高。截至2026年8月21日,全国已有高致病性禽流感确诊病例297例。这标志着该病毒首次被证实传播到澳大利亚本土哺乳动物。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
澳大利亚,南澳大利亚州,海滩港,位于阿德莱德以南约 310 公里处,沿海水域估计有 100,000 只长鼻海豹。
事件
澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感 病例。在南澳大利亚海滩港发现的一只死去的长鼻海狗身上发现了这种感染。
规模、影响与风险
H5N1 病毒已从 野生鸟类 传播到澳大利亚本土鸟类。目前全国有297例高致病性禽流感确诊病例。由于靠近 野生鸟类,海豹感染 H5N1 的风险很高。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚政府已确认该病例,并正在监测病毒的传播。没有证据表明海豹的死亡与其他海洋哺乳动物有关。在家禽养殖场或农业系统中尚未发现该病毒。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳洲確認首例哺乳動物感染H5N1禽流感 - 大纪元
News Brief
Australia confirmed its first case of a mammal infected with H5N1 avian influenza, with a long-nosed fur seal found dead in Beachport, South Australia. The virus, which has been spreading from wild birds to native birds, has not yet been detected in poultry farms or agricultural systems. The Australian government is monitoring the situation, noting that the seal's death is not linked to other marine mammals. The risk of H5N1 infection in seals is high due to their proximity to wild birds. As of August 21, 2026, there have been 297 confirmed cases of highly pathogenic avian influenza nationwide. This marks the first time the virus has been confirmed to spread to native mammals in Australia.
Original source text (Chinese)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳大利亚约千只小企鹅接种首剂H5N1 疫苗 - UA.NEWS

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报澳大利亚野生动物管理局和菲利普岛自然公园已针对野生小企鹅发起了两剂 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗接种 活动,过去一周已有约 1,000 只鸟类接受了第一剂疫苗。这项工作的目标是菲利普岛和圣基尔达的 5,000 只企鹅,使用了大约 10,000 剂 疫苗 剂量。菲利普岛 宿主 是世界上最大的小企鹅栖息地,拥有约 40,000 只鸟类;模型警告称,大型 H5N1 暴发 可能会导致 8,000-16,000 人死亡。该计划是在澳大利亚哺乳动物(南澳大利亚的长鼻海狗)中首次检测到 H5N1 病毒之后进行的,引发了人们对 跨物种溢出 风险的担忧。团队每晚为企鹅接种疫苗,同时最大程度地减少压力,并计划在 3-4 周内注射第二剂疫苗;当局指出,疫苗 可能会产生抗体,但其预防感染的能力尚未得到证实。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-23;据报告,该事件发生在过去一周,计划在 3-4 周内重新接种第二剂。
地点与对象
澳大利亚维多利亚州、菲利普岛和圣基尔达;野生小企鹅(Eudyptulaminor)种群。
事件
澳大利亚当局和菲利普岛自然公园针对野生小企鹅发起了两剂 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗接种 疫苗接种活动;状态已报告并正在进行。
规模、影响与风险
大约 1,000 只企鹅接受了第一剂疫苗;总目标是使用约 10,000 剂 疫苗 剂量覆盖菲利普岛和圣基尔达的 5,000 只家禽。菲利普岛 宿主 ~40,000 只小企鹅;最坏情况的 H5N1 暴发 模型表明有 8,000-16,000 人死亡。在南澳大利亚长鼻海狗中检测到 H5N1,这标志着澳大利亚哺乳动物种群中首次确认 跨物种溢出。
应对、状态与不确定性
每日晚间捕获和 疫苗接种 进行;计划在 3-4 周内重新接种第二剂。当局表示,疫苗 的目的是提高抗体,但对抗感染的功效尚不清楚;疫苗接种可以提供一些保护并帮助恢复。团队迅速采取行动,同时最大限度地减少由于密集的栖息地和病毒快速传播的风险而造成的压力。进一步更新可通过 UA.News 频道获取。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳大利亚约千只小企鹅接种首剂H5N1 疫苗 - UA.NEWS
News Brief
Australian wildlife authorities and Phillip Island Nature Parks have initiated a two-dose H5N1 avian influenza vaccination campaign for wild little penguins, with about 1,000 birds receiving the first dose over the past week. The effort targets 5,000 penguins across Phillip Island and St Kilda using roughly 10,000 vaccine doses. Phillip Island hosts the world’s largest little penguin colony of around 40,000 birds; modelling warns that a large H5N1 outbreak could kill 8,000–16,000 individuals. The program follows the first detection of H5N1 in an Australian mammal—a long-nosed fur seal in South Australia—raising concerns about spillover risk. Teams are vaccinating penguins nightly while minimizing stress, and a second dose is planned in 3–4 weeks; authorities note the vaccine may raise antibodies but its ability to prevent infection remains unproven.
Original source text (Chinese)

View five news elements
Time
Published 2026-08-23; event reported as occurring over the past week with second-dose recapture planned in 3–4 weeks.
Location and population
Victoria State, Phillip Island and St Kilda, Australia; wild little penguin (Eudyptula minor) population.
Event
Australian authorities and Phillip Island Nature Parks launched a two-dose H5N1 avian influenza vaccination campaign for wild little penguins; status is reported and ongoing.
Scale, impact and risk
Approximately 1,000 penguins received the first dose; total target is 5,000 birds across Phillip Island and St Kilda using about 10,000 vaccine doses. Phillip Island hosts ~40,000 little penguins; worst-case H5N1 outbreak modelling suggests 8,000–16,000 deaths. H5N1 was detected in a South Australian long-nosed fur seal, marking the first confirmed spillover into an Australian mammal population.
Response, status and uncertainty
Daily evening captures and vaccinations conducted; second dose recapture planned in 3–4 weeks. Authorities state vaccine aims to raise antibodies but efficacy against infection is unknown; vaccination may provide some protection and aid recovery. Teams act rapidly while minimizing stress due to dense rookeries and risk of rapid viral spread. Further updates are available via UA.News channels.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染H5N1禽流感病例 - 新西兰中文先驱网

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 23 日,南澳大利亚海滩港地区一只长鼻毛皮海豹检测呈阳性,澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感。自 6 月以来,在 野生鸟类 中检测到与 H5N1 相关的大规模海鸟死亡,但当局尚未报告在家禽或农业系统中出现病例。联邦农业部长朱莉·柯林斯警告说,一旦H5N1在环境中传播,重大损失就难以避免。南澳大利亚官员强调,由于海洋哺乳动物靠近 野生鸟类 栖息地,因此它们面临很高的风险。监测 正在持续监测海洋哺乳动物中潜在的进一步传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于 2026-08-24 10:54 NZST;据报道,该事件发生于 2026 年 8 月 23 日(星期日)。
地点与对象
澳大利亚,南澳大利亚州,Beachport沿海小镇(距离阿德莱德约310公里);海洋哺乳动物数量(估计有 100,000 只长鼻海狗)。
事件
澳大利亚确认首例 H5N1 禽流感 哺乳动物病例,患者是在 Beachport 发现的一只死亡的长鼻海狗。该报告基于南澳大利亚当局的官方确认和联邦声明。
规模、影响与风险
单一确诊哺乳动物病例(一头长鼻海狗死亡)。没有人间病例报告。自2026年6月起在澳大利亚野生鸟类中检测到H5N1,本月出现大规模海鸟死亡。截至报告日期,未在家禽或农业系统中检测到。由于与 野生鸟类 密切接触,海洋哺乳动物的风险被描述为高。
应对、状态与不确定性
南澳大利亚当局和联邦农业部长朱莉·柯林斯承认了这一发现,并警告说,H5N1 在环境中的传播导致重大损失难以避免。监测 正在进一步传播到海洋哺乳动物。没有家禽感染的证据报告。传播 的动态和 跨物种溢出 对其他物种的潜力仍然存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染H5N1禽流感病例 - 新西兰中文先驱网
News Brief
Australia confirmed its first mammal infection with H5N1 avian influenza after a long-nosed fur seal tested positive in South Australia’s Beachport region on August 23, 2026. The discovery follows large-scale seabird die-offs linked to H5N1 detected in wild birds since June, though authorities report no cases in domestic poultry or agricultural systems. Federal Agriculture Minister Julie Collins warned that once H5N1 circulates in the environment, significant losses become hard to avoid. South Australian officials emphasized the high risk to marine mammals due to their proximity to wild bird habitats. Surveillance is ongoing to monitor potential further spread among marine mammals.
Original source text (Chinese)

× 澳大利亚确认首例哺乳动物感染H5N1禽流感病例 By 新西兰中文先驱网· 2026年08月24日 10:54 禽流感 分享 听新闻▶0:00/0:00✕ 顶部 侨报讯 澳大利亚周日确认首例哺乳动物感染H5N1禽流感病例,在南澳大利亚州一只长吻毛皮海豹体内检测到这种致命病毒。 据路透社报道,澳大利亚自6月首次出现H5N1禽流感后,疫情持续扩大。本月,该国首次出现大规模海鸟死亡事件,标志着疫情进一步升级。病毒最初在迁徙海鸟中被发现,随后扩散至当地野生动物。 南澳大利亚州有关部门确认,在距离州首府阿德莱德约310公里的海滨小镇比奇波特(Beachport)发现了这只死亡海豹。这只海豹没有明显外伤。当地水域估计约有10万只同类海豹。 澳大利亚联邦农业部长朱莉·柯林斯(Julie Collins)在电视讲话中表示,这只海豹的死亡“似乎与其他患病或死亡的海洋哺乳动物没有关联”。她说,一旦H5N1禽流感开始在自然环境中传播,“就不可能避免出现重大损失”。 澳大利亚是全球最后一个报告禽流感疫情的大陆。不过,2025年年底,病毒已经在澳大利亚的亚南极领地赫德岛(Heard Island)被发现。据当地媒体报道,病毒已导致岛上超过1.3万只幼年海豹死亡。 南澳大利亚州首席兽医官斯凯·弗鲁恩(Skye Fruean)表示,海豹感染H5N1的风险较高,因为它们经常生活在海岸岩礁附近,与野生鸟类距离很近。 她说,这些海豹“毫无疑问会在环境中接触到非常高水平的病毒”。 不过,澳大利亚联邦政府表示,目前尚未在该国的家禽或农业生产体系中发现H5N1。政府周五公布的数据显示,全国目前已确认297例禽流感病例。 随着H5N1从野生鸟类向哺乳动物扩散,澳大利亚当局正密切关注病毒是否进一步传播至更多海洋哺乳动物。 相关阅读: 新西兰发现第三例H5禽流感病例 新州致命禽流感疫情持续扩散,本土鸟类首次确诊!全澳确诊总数升至215例 H5禽流感在澳扩散,海滩现大量死鸟,专家忧心“进入下一阶段” 为防禽流感,55万只散养鸡进入封闭管理,超市呼吁勿抢购囤积 新西兰确认第二例H5N1禽流感!本土鸟类中招,家禽业高度戒备! 禽流感病例首现,专家警告这类物种面临巨大风险!环保部正在行动! 新西兰确诊首例H5禽流感病例!鸡蛋要涨价了? View five news elements
Time
Published 2026-08-24 10:54 NZST; event reported as occurring on 2026-08-23 (Sunday).
Location and population
Australia, South Australia state, Beachport coastal town (~310 km from Adelaide); marine mammal population (estimated 100,000 long-nosed fur seals).
Event
Australia confirmed its first mammal case of H5N1 avian influenza in a long-nosed fur seal found dead in Beachport. The report is based on official confirmation by South Australian authorities and federal statements.
Scale, impact and risk
Single confirmed mammal case (one dead long-nosed fur seal). No human cases reported. H5N1 detected in wild birds in Australia since June 2026, with large-scale seabird die-offs this month. No detection in domestic poultry or agricultural systems as of report date. Risk to marine mammals described as high due to close contact with wild birds.
Response, status and uncertainty
South Australian authorities and federal Agriculture Minister Julie Collins acknowledged the detection and warned that H5N1 spread in the environment makes major losses difficult to avoid. Surveillance is ongoing for further spread to marine mammals. No evidence of poultry infection reported. Uncertainty remains regarding transmission dynamics and potential for spillover to other species.

南澳海狗染H5N1禽流感澳洲大陆首有哺乳动物确诊|即时新闻|国际|on.cc东网 - on.cc東網

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报8 月 19 日,南澳大利亚州海滩港发现一只长鼻毛皮海豹死亡,检测结果呈阳性,澳大利亚大陆出现首例哺乳动物 H5N1 禽流感 确诊病例。联邦农业部长称这一发现令人痛苦但并不令人惊讶,并指出该动物可能是通过接触受感染的 野生鸟类 或共享环境而感染该病毒。全国范围内,已报告检测到 298 起 禽流感,其中南澳大利亚州占大多数。人与人之间的 传播 风险评估为低,当局敦促公众避免患病或死亡的鸟类并报告目击事件。作为应对措施的一部分,南澳大利亚目前正在评估美国开发的实验性 疫苗 是否可能用于当地哺乳动物。
查看新闻五要素
时间
活动日期:2026年8月19日;发布日期:2026 年 8 月 23 日。
地点与对象
澳大利亚南澳大利亚海滩港;全国范围包括南澳大利亚州、维多利亚州、新南威尔士州、西澳大利亚州、塔斯马尼亚州和昆士兰州。
事件
在 Beachport 发现的一只长鼻海豹尸体检测出 H5N1 禽流感 呈阳性,这是澳大利亚大陆首例确诊的哺乳动物感染。由南澳大利亚政府报告并由联邦农业部长引用。
规模、影响与风险
一例确诊的哺乳动物病例(长鼻海狗);全国累计298例禽流感病例(南澳大利亚州183例,维多利亚州86例,新南威尔士州11例,西澳大利亚州10例,塔斯马尼亚州7例,昆士兰州1例)。人类 传播 风险仍然较低;当局建议避免接触病鸟或死鸟并报告目击事件。对包括濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮在内的海洋哺乳动物存在高风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
南澳大利亚正在与联邦政府合作评估美国实验 疫苗 在澳大利亚哺乳动物中的使用。调查正在进行中;官员们承认无法阻止 野生鸟类 中的病毒传播,并预计如果 野生鸟类 死亡率 上升,海洋哺乳动物的检测量将会增加。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
南澳海狗染H5N1禽流感澳洲大陆首有哺乳动物确诊|即时新闻|国际|on.cc东网 - on.cc東網
News Brief
Australia’s mainland has recorded its first confirmed case of H5N1 avian influenza in a mammal after a long-nosed fur seal found dead on 19 August in Beachport, South Australia, tested positive. The federal Agriculture Minister described the finding as distressing yet unsurprising, noting the animal likely acquired the virus through contact with infected wild birds or shared environment. Nationally, 298 avian influenza detections have been reported, with South Australia accounting for the majority. Human-to-human transmission risk is assessed as low, and authorities urge the public to avoid sick or dead birds and report sightings. South Australia is now evaluating a US-developed experimental vaccine for potential use in local mammals as part of the response.

View five news elements
Time
Event date: 19 August 2026; Publication date: 23 August 2026.
Location and population
Beachport, South Australia, Australia; national scope including South Australia, Victoria, New South Wales, Western Australia, Tasmania, and Queensland.
Event
A long-nosed fur seal found dead in Beachport tested positive for H5N1 avian influenza, marking Australia’s mainland first confirmed mammalian infection. Reported by the South Australian government and cited by the federal Minister for Agriculture.
Scale, impact and risk
One confirmed mammalian case (long-nosed fur seal); national cumulative 298 avian influenza cases (South Australia 183, Victoria 86, New South Wales 11, Western Australia 10, Tasmania 7, Queensland 1). Human transmission risk remains low; authorities advise avoiding sick or dead birds and reporting sightings. High risk to marine mammals including endangered Australian sea lions.
Response, status and uncertainty
South Australia is collaborating with the federal government to assess a US experimental vaccine for use in Australian mammals. Investigation ongoing; officials acknowledge inability to stop viral spread among wild birds and expect increased marine mammal detections if wild bird mortality rises.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

南澳州海獅無外傷死亡 檢出H5N1禽流感病毒 - 新唐人電視台

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026年8月23日,澳大利亚当局在哺乳动物宿主中确认了首例H5N1 禽流感病例,这是一只在南澳大利亚海滩港发现死亡的长鼻海狗,没有外伤。这标志着首次在澳大利亚的哺乳动物中检测到该病毒。暴发 自 6 月以来一直在持续,全国已有 297 例确诊病例,主要影响鸟类。尽管对人类的风险仍然很低,但官员们敦促公众避免接触患病或死亡的鸟类,并报告任何目击事件。该病毒已从候鸟传播到当地物种,引发人们对其可能对自然环境造成重大损失的担忧。该事件凸显了 H5N1 在澳大利亚日益严重的威胁以及持续监测和提高公众意识的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
南澳大利亚州,特别是海滨小镇海滩港 (Beachport),该地区约有 100,000 只长鼻海狗。
事件
一只长鼻海狗被发现死亡,没有外伤,测试证实存在H5N1 禽流感病毒,这是澳大利亚首例哺乳动物宿主感染该病毒的病例。
规模、影响与风险
这是澳大利亚首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 病毒的确诊病例。该病毒一直在鸟类中传播,全国确诊病例超过297例,但家禽或农业部门尚未报告病例。尽管当局建议避免接触病鸟或死鸟,但对人类的风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局正在调查此案,并向公众发出警告,避免接触受影响的鸟类。目前情况正在接受审查,病毒在自然环境中的传播令人担忧。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
南澳州海獅無外傷死亡 檢出H5N1禽流感病毒 - 新唐人電視台
News Brief
On August 23, 2026, Australian authorities confirmed the first case of H5N1 avian influenza in a mammalian host, a long-nosed fur seal found dead in Beachport, South Australia, without external injuries. This marks the first time the virus has been detected in a mammal in Australia. The outbreak has been ongoing since June, with 297 confirmed cases nationwide, primarily affecting birds. While the risk to humans remains low, officials urge the public to avoid contact with sick or dead birds and report any sightings. The virus has spread from migratory birds to local species, raising concerns about its potential to cause significant losses in natural environments. The incident highlights the growing threat of H5N1 in Australia and the need for continued monitoring and public awareness.

View five news elements
Time
The supplied evidence does not clearly report the event time in verified English.
Location and population
South Australia, specifically in the coastal town of Beachport, with approximately 100,000 long-nosed fur seals in the region.
Event
A long-nosed fur seal was found dead without external injuries, and tests confirmed the presence of H5N1 avian influenza virus, marking the first case of this virus in a mammalian host in Australia.
Scale, impact and risk
This is the first confirmed case of H5N1 in a mammal in Australia. The virus has been spreading among birds, with over 297 confirmed cases nationwide, but no cases have been reported in poultry or agricultural sectors. The risk to humans remains low, though authorities advise avoiding contact with sick or dead birds.
Response, status and uncertainty
Authorities are investigating the case and have issued warnings to the public to avoid contact with affected birds. The situation is under review, and the spread of the virus in natural environments is a concern.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

家禽业结构影响禽流感的抵御能力

Poultry industry structure affects resilience to avian flu
新闻简报一项新的研究表明,家禽业的结构显着影响其对 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 的抵御能力。这项由皇家兽医学院和剑桥大学进行的研究强调,规划决策、基础设施和财务风险管理等因素在农场应对 HPAI 暴发 方面发挥着至关重要的作用。2025-2026年HPAI季节,英国记录了100例确诊的暴发,全部涉及H5N1病毒变种,其中英国数量最多。欧洲 16 个国家的养殖家禽中发现 311 例 HPAI 暴发,其中波兰最多,有 143 例。2026 年迄今为止,欧洲的 野生鸟类 已出现 2,672 例 暴发,其中 2,630 例检测到 H5N1 血清型。该研究强调,仅生物安全是不够的,整个行业的结构性因素对于预防禽流感也同样重要。欧洲的高致病性禽流感疫情正在持续监测中,过去四个星期内仅报告了商业家禽中的一例新的 暴发。然而,野生鸟类 中 H5N1 的存在表明,随着秋季的临近,H5N1 病毒可能会卷土重来。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于2026年8月25日。该研究涉及2025年10月1日开始的2025-2026年高致病性禽流感季节。截至2026年8月19日报告了欧洲高致病性禽流感暴发,英国自2026年5月1日以来记录了最后一起家禽病例。
地点与对象
该研究重点关注英国的家禽业,特别是英格兰、苏格兰、威尔士和北爱尔兰。它还提到了欧洲,包括瑞典和波兰,以及多个国家的 野生鸟类。
事件
皇家兽医学院和剑桥大学的一项研究发现,规划、基础设施和财务风险管理等因素显着影响家禽业对 HPAI 暴发 的抵御能力。研究还指出,H5N1 HPAI 病毒已在欧洲各地的 暴发 中得到证实,其中波兰的病例数最多。
规模、影响与风险
2025-2026年HPAI季节,英国确诊了100起暴发,全部涉及H5N1病毒。欧洲 16 个国家的养殖家禽中记录了 311 例 HPAI 暴发,其中波兰最多,有 143 例。2026 年迄今为止,欧洲的 野生鸟类 已出现 2,672 起 暴发,其中 2,630 多起 暴发 中检测到 H5N1 血清型。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究强调了规划、基础设施和保险在管理 HPAI 暴发 中的重要性。欧洲的高致病性禽流感疫情正在持续监测中,自 2026 年 5 月 1 日以来,英国未报告新的家禽病例,过去 4 周欧洲商业家禽中仅报告一例新的 暴发 病例。野生鸟类 中 H5N1 的存在表明,随着秋季的临近,H5N1 病毒可能会卷土重来。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Poultry industry structure affects resilience to avian flu

News Brief
A new study reveals that the structure of the poultry industry significantly affects its resilience to highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI). The research, conducted by the Royal Veterinary College and the University of Cambridge, highlights that factors such as planning decisions, infrastructure, and financial risk management play a crucial role in how farms respond to HPAI outbreaks. In the 2025-2026 HPAI season, the U.K. recorded 100 confirmed outbreaks, all involving the H5N1 virus variant, with the highest number in England. Europe saw 311 HPAI outbreaks in farmed poultry across 16 countries, with Poland leading at 143 cases. Wild birds in Europe have experienced 2,672 outbreaks so far in 2026, with the H5N1 serotype detected in over 2,630 of these. The study emphasizes that biosecurity alone is insufficient, and industry-wide structural factors are equally important in protecting against avian flu. The HPAI situation in Europe is under ongoing monitoring, with only one new outbreak in commercial poultry reported in the past four weeks. However, the presence of H5N1 in wild birds suggests a potential resurgence as autumn approaches.
Original source text (English)
___ × Market Information Top Poultry Companies Expert Insights Blogs Regions Magazines Sign In Poultry Meat Egg Poultry Health Sustainability Poultry Future Chicken Marketing Summit Poultry Tech Summit Broilers & Turkeys Diseases & Health Avian Influenza Poultry industry structure affects resilience to avian flu A new study reveals how a wide range of factors impact the sector’s capabilities in preparing for, and responding to, outbreaks of highly pathogenic avian influenza. Jackie Linden August 26, 2026 Alena Ozihilevich | Bigstock Article Summary Poultry industry resilience to highly pathogenic avian influenza depends not only on biosecurity practices but also on broader structural factors including farm planning decisions, location determined by local authorities, infrastructure, and access to avian flu insurance. A Royal Veterinary College study found that limited insurance options and planning constraints significantly affect how farms manage and recover from HPAI outbreaks. Planning and location decisions by local authorities significantly impact farm resilience, as they must balance farmer interests with community and environmental considerations Insurance availability is limited, making financial risk management a critical business challenge during outbreaks and recovery Each poultry sub-sector (meat, eggs, and game birds) faces distinct HPAI-related challenges requiring tailored resilience strategies Biosecurity alone is insufficient — industry-wide structural factors play an equally important role in protecting against avian flu Europe recorded 311 HPAI outbreaks in farmed poultry across 16 countries in 2026, with Poland leading at 143 cases Just one new highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) outbreak involving poultry has occurred in Europe since the end of July — further evidence of a seasonal lull in infections during the summer months in the northern hemisphere. However, reports of new infections in wild birds across the continent over this period suggests the H5N1 HPAI virus could return with a vengeance as the autumn/fall approaches. It is widely accepted that the maintenance of high standards of biosecurity help to protect poultry on individual farms from HPAI. However, a recently published study reveals that more general factors in the industry also have a role to play. Among those factors impacting the poultry sector in Great Britain are planning, infrastructure, and financial risk, according to the Royal Veterinary College (RVC). From the RVC’s study in cooperation with the University of Cambridge, planning decisions and insurance emerge as key issues affecting resilience to HPAI. Where a poultry farm is located depends on a decision by the local authority, which aims to balance the interests of the farmer with those of the wider community as well as environmental considerations, the study found. With limited options for avian flu insurance, financial risk is also an important business consideration, particularly as producers consider how they will manage firstly the outbreak, and then the recovery of the business. From the study, the researchers also noted that each poultry industry sub-sector — poultry meat, eggs, and game birds — face slightly different HPAI-related challenges. The RVC’s research paper — entitled “Negotiating security and resilience: planning conundrums for avian influenza control in Great Britain’s poultry systems” by M. Hennessey and others — is published in the journal Environmental Research: Food Systems. In the 2025-2026 HPAI “season” (starting October 1, 2025), there were 100 confirmed outbreaks in the poultry in the United Kingdom (U.K.), according to the government agriculture department, Defra. All involved the H5N1 HPAI virus serotype. Of the total, 79 outbreaks occurred in England, nine in Scotland, seven in Wales, and five in Northern Ireland. No new cases in poultry have been recorded in the U.K. since the start of May. HPAI situation in Europe’s domestic birds Over the past four weeks, just one HPAI outbreak in commercial poultry has been reported in Europe. Affected was a small commercial egg farm in the southwest of Sweden, according to an official notification from the national veterinary agency to the World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH). Presence of the H5N1 virus variant was confirmed in the flock of 145 birds in the municipality of Tanum in mid-August. This was Sweden’s third outbreak in poultry so far this year, based on the Animal Disease Information System (as of August 19). This database from the European Commission (EC) monitors listed animal diseases in European Union member states and selected adjacent countries (but not Great Britain). So far this year, there have been 311 HPAI outbreaks in farmed poultry across Europe, with one or more outbreaks registered by 16 countries. All outbreaks have involved the H5N1 virus variant. Poland’s total is the highest at 143, followed by Germany (40), France (20), and Italy (19). During the whole of 2025, 729 outbreaks in this category occurred in 23 countries. The EC’s System records HPAI outbreaks involving backyard poultry, ornamental flocks, zoos, and similar premises in a separate category as “captive birds.” With no new cases in the past month, the region’s outbreak total in this population so far in 2026 remains unchanged at 119 in 15 countries. Sporadic new cases in European wild birds Since July 31, a total of 19 new HPAI outbreaks involving the wild bird population have been recorded in five of the region’s states by the EC System. Of these, 13 were confirmed in the Netherlands, while Finland, Germany, Norway, and Sweden account for the remaining cases. These bring the outbreak total in Europe’s wild population for the year to date to 2,672 (as of August 19), with one or more cases reported in 32 countries. The H5N1 serotype was detected in more than 2,630 of these outbreaks. For the other outbreaks, the virus was identified as belonging to the H5 “family.” Not included in the EC’s System are disease developments in Great Britain. Earlier this month, a wild goose found dead in north Wales tested positive for the H5N1 virus variant, according to a recent WOAH notification. View our continuing coverage of the global avian influenza situation in poultry, and on disease developments in the U.S. dairy sector. 科学研讨会“家禽中 H9N2 和 IBV 双重感染:流行病学挑战和综合预防解决方案” - Vietnam.vn

Scientific workshop “H9N2 and IBV co-infection in poultry: Epidemiological challenges and comprehensive prevention solutions” - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 21 日,关于家禽中 H9N2 和 IBV 混合感染的科学研讨会在越南河内越南农业科学院兽医学院举行。该活动是与 VINODA 和越南建明公司合作举办的,旨在应对流行病学挑战,并为家禽养殖中的这些感染提供全面的预防解决方案。Dang Huu Anh和Wei Shen等专家发表了演讲,讨论了H9N2和IBV的特征、它们的混合感染现象以及疾病控制的先进生物方法。研讨会还包括问答环节以及学生、讲师和企业代表之间的热烈讨论,以实践见解强化课堂知识。该活动是建明教育系列的一部分,旨在庆祝兽医学院成立 70 周年并促进学术界与工业界之间的合作。没有报告具体病例数或风险水平,但研讨会强调了生物安全解决方案在管理家禽疾病方面的重要性。Vietnam.vn 于 2026 年 8 月 24 日发布了该新闻报道,强调了该活动的教育和专业价值。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研讨会于2026年8月21日举行,新闻报道于2026年8月24日发布。研讨会当天的活动持续了3.5个小时。
地点与对象
研讨会在越南河内越南农业科学院兽医学院 409 室举行。会议成员包括教师、企业代表、专家、讲师以及大批兽医学院的学生。
事件
越南农业科学院兽医学院与 VINODA 和越南建明公司合作举办了关于家禽中 H9N2 和 IBV 混合感染的科学研讨会。该活动的重点是家禽养殖中这些感染的流行病学挑战和预防解决方案。
规模、影响与风险
研讨会提供了有关家禽中 H9N2 和 IBV 双重感染的最新知识,强调了了解这些病毒对于疾病预防和控制的重要性。没有报告具体病例数、死亡数或风险水平,但该活动强调了家禽养殖需要先进的生物安全解决方案。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研讨会是建明教育系列的一部分,旨在加强学术培训、科学研究和商业实践之间的联系。它包括讨论、演示和迷你游戏,重点是实践知识和协作。该活动被确认为科学和教育活动,不存在未解决的信息或来源限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Scientific workshop “H9N2 and IBV co-infection in poultry: Epidemiological challenges and comprehensive prevention solutions” - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
A scientific workshop on the co-infection of H9N2 and IBV in poultry was held on August 21, 2026, at the Faculty of Veterinary Medicine, Vietnam Academy of Agriculture, in Hanoi, Vietnam. The event, organized in collaboration with VINODA and Kemin Vietnam, aimed to address epidemiological challenges and provide comprehensive prevention solutions for these infections in poultry farming. It featured presentations by experts, including Dang Huu Anh and Wei Shen, who discussed the characteristics of H9N2 and IBV, their co-infection phenomena, and advanced biological methods for disease control. The workshop also included a Q&A session and lively discussions among students, lecturers, and business representatives, reinforcing classroom knowledge with practical insights. The event was part of the Kemin Education Series, celebrating the 70th anniversary of the Faculty of Veterinary Medicine and promoting collaboration between academia and industry. No specific case counts or risk levels were reported, but the workshop emphasized the importance of biosafety solutions in managing poultry diseases. The news report was published on August 24, 2026, by Vietnam.vn, highlighting the educational and professional value of the event.

墨尔本 返回旧版首页 下载yeeyi App 商务合作 帮助 新版反馈 发布信息 登录/注册 新闻亿部落分类信息用户 租房招聘二手家具搬家清洁屋顶维护宠物二手教材 本地资讯 房屋租赁 房屋交易 车辆交易 求职招聘 二手市场 生意买卖 宠物交易 会计税务 物流搬运 清洁通渠 园艺绿化 水管电工 保姆月嫂 建筑家装 驾校招生 贷 款 生活服务 首页 澳洲 正文 维州调整SRL东线方案 / 专家称食品未来或上涨 / 墨尔本Monash发现H5N1禽流感 2 天前来源: 澳大利亚大华时代503分享到: 7收藏6赞 维州调整SRL东线方案 维州大型基建项目郊区铁路环线(Suburban Rail Loop,SRL)长期因成本上涨和财政压力受到争议。新任州长Ben Carroll上任后决定继续推进项目,但通过削减部分设计内容和调整管理方式,降低建设成本。 Carroll于8月25日宣布,SRL East(东线)将削减约20亿澳元成本。其中约10亿澳元来自取消部分“非必要”设计,另外10亿澳元节省目标将通过独立技术评估寻找。SRL East原预算最高约345亿澳元,调整后目标降至约323亿澳元。 根据调整方案,Box Hill和Glen Waverley车站原计划建设的地下换乘设施将取消,改为地面换乘方式;Clayton车站第二个出入口将取消,Burwood和Cheltenham车站复杂的人行天桥设计也将调整。此外,部分停车设施和社区配套项目也会缩减。 除了工程调整,政府还将重组项目管理架构,撤销独立运作的郊区铁路环线管理局(Suburban Rail Loop Authority),相关职能并入维州基础设施交付机构(Victorian Infrastructure Delivery Authority)。 Carroll表示,已经签署的合同和现有工程不会暂停,但在独立技术评估完成前,政府不会签署新的建设合同。 专家称食品未来或上涨 能源专家表示,伊朗冲突带来的通胀压力目前还没有明显反映在澳洲食品价格中,但随着燃料、肥料等农业成本上升,未来食品价格可能面临上涨压力。 麦考瑞大学高级讲师Lurion De Mello表示,霍尔木兹海峡是全球重要石油和化肥运输通道,冲突已经推高部分生产成本。不过,从农场到超市需要一定时间,影响可能要到未来几个月甚至明年才逐渐体现。 目前澳洲食品价格上涨主要不是由伊朗冲突导致。截至6月的一年内,食品和非酒精饮料价格上涨3.3%,其中羊肉和山羊肉上涨14.9%,牛肉和小牛肉上涨11.9%,主要原因是海外需求增加。 De Mello表示,未来受影响较大的可能是依赖进口的食品,例如部分罐头、豆类和意大利面等,因为运输和生产成本增加。不过,澳洲本身是小麦等农产品出口国,全球粮食价格波动对面包等日常食品的影响相对有限。他也提醒,消费者无需提前大量囤货,真正的价格变化还需要观察未来几个月的数据。 墨尔本Monash发现H5N1禽流感 Monash市议会8月25日表示,当地已经确认出现H5N1禽流感。卫生部门目前认为这种病毒对普通居民构成的风险仍然较低,但市民需要避免直接接触患病或死亡的野生动物。 居民不要触摸野生鸟类、羽毛、粪便或鸟蛋,也应让儿童和宠物远离相关区域。遇到行动异常、无法飞行或已经死亡的野生动物时,不建议自行检查。 维州把同一地点出现五只或以上患病、死亡野鸟视为需要报告的禽流感事件。即使只有一只海鸟、水鸟、滨鸟、猛禽或海洋哺乳动物出现异常,也应拨打1800 675 888报告。 如果动物位于Monash市议会管理的土地上,可致电9518 3555安排处理。必须清理自家土地上的死鸟时,应穿长袖衣物和手套,使用铲子或夹具,将动物双层密封后放入红盖垃圾桶,并彻底清洗双手、衣物和工具。 维州流感和新冠感染开始回升 维州首席卫生官麦克尔内(Caroline McElnay)发布最新健康警报,提醒居民近期流感和新冠感染数量正在增加,而且今年的上升时间晚于往年。 维州卫生厅表示,呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)病例在7月达到高峰后仍处于较高水平,因呼吸道疾病前往医疗机构就诊的人数也在增加。 幼儿、孕妇、老年人、免疫力较弱者及患有基础疾病的人,出现重症和住院的风险相对较高。卫生部门表示,现在接种流感疫苗仍不算晚,部分居民可以免费接种。 居民出现发烧、咳嗽、喉咙痛或全身疲劳等症状时,应尽量留在家中。维州卫生厅还建议勤洗手、遮掩咳嗽和喷嚏,并可通过家庭医生远程问诊、Nurse-on-Call或维州虚拟急诊寻求帮助。 新州老虎机强制使用人脸识别 新南威尔士州政府近日公布老虎机监管改革方案,未来博彩场所将强制使用人脸识别技术,并建立全州统一的赌博排除登记系统,以识别被禁止进入博彩区的人员。 改革还包括禁止VIP赌博计划,加强对博彩推广的限制。 新州政府表示,新措施旨在减少问题赌博带来的伤害,并提高博彩场所监管能力。政府希望通过技术手段,让高风险赌博行为更容易被发现和干预。 不过,反赌博组织认为改革力度仍然不足。Wesley Mission负责人Stu Cameron表示,政府仍没有采取足够强有力的行动解决老虎机带来的长期问题。 Ballarat PopCon将于9月回归 据Ballarat市议会8月25日消息,年度流行文化活动PopCon Ballarat将于9月27日举行,地点设在Ballarat Library和Civic Hall,活动时间为上午11时至下午4时。活动免费开放,面向各年龄段的动漫、游戏、电影、科幻和奇幻文化爱好者。 今年活动现场将设置超过50个摊位,并提供角色扮演(Cosplay)、桌游、游戏体验、乐高展示、艺术创作和手工工作坊等内容。主办方希望通过活动,让当地居民展示创意,并与兴趣相同的人交流。 活动亮点之一是今年新增的真人角色扮演体验,由当地团体Imagined Realms提供。此外,Cosplay比赛将在当天下午2时30分举行,没有报名参赛的参与者也可以参加服装巡游,展示自己的角色造型。 PopCon由Ballarat青年组织Sonika和Ballarat市议会青年服务团队共同策划。活动自2017年推出以来逐渐扩大规模,并于2025年转移到更大的Ballarat Library和Civic Hall举办,成为维州西部地区较受欢迎的免费流行文化活动之一。 Woolworths提前四个月摆圣诞商品 距离圣诞节还有四个月,悉尼Mascot一家Woolworths门店已经开始摆放圣诞主题食品和商品。提前出现的节日货架迅速引起消费者讨论。 支持者认为,提前上架可以延长采购时间,也能让喜欢圣诞节的家庭更早准备。不过也有反对者则表示,节日商品在8月出现过早,可能让12月真正到来时失去新鲜感。 类似争议在澳洲零售业并不少见,包括复活节十字面包等季节性商品也经常提前数月销售。对大型零售商而言,延长销售周期可以测试需求,并减少节日前集中备货的压力。 目前报道明确涉及的是悉尼Mascot门店,并不能代表所有Woolworths门店已经同步上架,各地销售时间仍可能有所不同。
