B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳大利亚野生动物园将为100只鸟接种H5N1 疫苗 - UA.NEWS

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026 年 8 月,南澳大利亚的一个野生动物园,特别是阿德莱德山的克莱兰野生动物园,宣布计划为大约 100 只圈养本地鸟类接种 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗。据澳大利亚广播公司报道,这一举措使该公园成为南澳大利亚第一个获得联邦政府批准使用 疫苗 的公园。这些鸟类包括濒临灭绝的物种,如橙腹鹦鹉和蒙面田凫,以及其他脆弱的鸟类。疫苗接种 计划是防止 H5N1(一种高致病性菌株)传播的更广泛努力的一部分。作为这些准备工作的一部分,该公园已获得联邦资金来升级鸟类围栏。然而,疫苗 对不同鸟类的有效性尚未得到 100% 的确定,并且不能提供完整的保护。第二次给药后将观察鸟类。动物园 SA 组织还在审查其他设施的 疫苗接种 批准,并且针对菲利普岛小企鹅栖息地的单独 疫苗接种 计划正在进行中。来源为UA.News的部分报道,信息基于公园公告和澳大利亚广播公司报道。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
南澳大利亚州阿德莱德山,大约有 100 只圈养本土鸟类,包括橙腹鹦鹉和蒙面田凫等濒临灭绝的物种,以及其他脆弱的鸟类。
事件
据澳大利亚广播公司报道,南澳大利亚的一个野生动物园计划为大约 100 只圈养的本土鸟类接种 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗。该公园是南澳大利亚第一个获得联邦政府批准用于 疫苗 用途的公园。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗接种 计划旨在保护鸟类免受 H5N1 禽流感(一种高致病性菌株)的感染。作为准备工作的一部分,该公园已获得联邦资金来升级鸟类围栏。然而,疫苗 对不同鸟类的有效性尚未得到 100% 的确定,并且不能提供完整的保护。第二次给药后将观察鸟类。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗接种 计划是防止 H5N1 禽流感 传播的更广泛努力的一部分。联邦政府已批准使用疫苗,公园正在实施。然而,疫苗 对各种鸟类的有效性尚未得到充分证实。动物园 SA 组织还在审查其他设施的 疫苗接种 批准,并且针对菲利普岛小企鹅栖息地的单独 疫苗接种 计划正在进行中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳大利亚野生动物园将为100只鸟接种H5N1 疫苗 - UA.NEWS
News Brief
In August 2026, a wildlife park in South Australia, specifically the Cleland Wildlife Park in the Adelaide Hills, announced plans to vaccinate approximately 100 captive native birds against H5N1 avian influenza. This initiative, reported by the Australian Broadcasting Corporation, makes the park the first in South Australia to receive federal government approval for the use of this vaccine. The birds include endangered species such as the orange-bellied parrot and the masked lapwing, as well as other vulnerable bird species. The vaccination program is part of broader efforts to prevent the spread of H5N1, which is a highly pathogenic strain. The park has received federal funding to upgrade bird enclosures as part of these preparations. However, the vaccine's effectiveness on different bird species has not been confirmed with 100% certainty, and it does not provide complete protection. Birds will be observed after the second dose. The Zoos SA organization is also reviewing vaccination approvals for other facilities, and a separate vaccination plan for the Phillip Island little penguin colony is underway. The source is a partial report from UA.News, and the information is based on the park's announcement and the Australian Broadcasting Corporation's coverage.
Original source text (Chinese)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

澳大利亚为数千只小蓝企鹅接种H5N1禽流感疫苗 - 搜狐网

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报澳大利亚维多利亚州针对菲利普岛和墨尔本圣基尔达地区的小企鹅启动了一项针对性的H5N1 禽流感 疫苗接种计划,旨在保护5000只鸟类。截至 8 月 23 日晚上,作为应对高致病性 H5N1 威胁活动的一部分,已有 1,000 只企鹅接受了两剂 疫苗 中的第一剂。鉴于该病毒对野生动物 死亡率 和人畜共患 死亡率 的潜在影响,当局将风险描述为“高”。该倡议体现了一项积极主动的公共卫生措施,旨在保护标志性物种,同时减少 跨物种溢出 对人类和其他动物的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-25;据报告,该事件仍在进行中,第一剂给药将于 2026 年 8 月 23 日晚上完成。
地点与对象
澳大利亚,维多利亚州;目标种群:菲利普岛和圣基尔达(墨尔本)的 5,000 只小企鹅(Eudyptulaminor)。
事件
澳大利亚当局针对小企鹅发起了针对高致病性 H5N1 禽流感 的 疫苗接种 运动,以降低人畜共患风险。
规模、影响与风险
计划为疫苗接种接种5,000只企鹅;截至2026-08-23,已有1,000只接种了两剂疫苗中的第一剂。由于H5N1对野生动物的威胁及潜在的人兽共患病风险,该项目风险被评估为高。
应对、状态与不确定性
活动正在进行中;疫苗接种 正在进行中;没有报告企鹅的不良事件或病例检测。两剂注射计划的完成和长期免疫仍存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
澳大利亚为数千只小蓝企鹅接种H5N1禽流感疫苗 - 搜狐网
News Brief
Australia’s Victoria state has launched a targeted H5N1 avian influenza vaccination program for little penguins on Phillip Island and in Melbourne’s St Kilda area, aiming to protect 5,000 birds. By August 23 evening, 1,000 penguins had received the first of two vaccine doses as part of the campaign to counter the threat of highly pathogenic H5N1. Authorities describe the risk as high given the virus’s potential for wildlife mortality and zoonotic transmission. The initiative reflects a proactive public-health measure to safeguard an iconic species while reducing spillover risks to humans and other animals.

5,000 penguins planned for vaccination; 1,000 received the first of two doses by 2026-08-23. Risk cited as high due to H5N1 threat to wildlife and potential zoonotic spillover.

Campaign underway; vaccination ongoing; no reported adverse events or case detections in penguins. Uncertainty remains regarding completion of two-dose schedule and long-term immunity.
原文 / Source
Published: 2026-08-26  |  Publisher: Bing News RSS
B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Avian flu affects 77,700 SD birds, raising state’s 4-year outbreak total to 8.1 million

Avian flu affects 77,700 SD birds, raising state’s 4-year outbreak total to 8.1 million
新闻简报Avian flu has returned to South Dakota, with federal officials confirming detections in two flocks this month. The highly pathogenic avian influenza virus, specifically H5N1, struck a commercial turkey flock of 74,900 in Beadle County on August 12 and a backyard flock of 2,800 birds in Brule County on Thursday. The standard response is to euthanize the entire affected flock, which is a common practice when the disease is confirmed. This outbreak adds to a 4-year total of 8.1 million cases in the state, highlighting the rapid spread and high mortality rate of the virus. The article does not mention any additional measures or ongoing investigations beyond the standard response, indicating that the situation is being managed through routine procedures. The outbreak raises concerns about the impact on bird populations and potential public health risks, though no human infections are reported in the supplied evidence.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on August 26, 2026. The avian flu outbreak in South Dakota was reported to have affected 77,700 birds in the state, with the 4-year outbreak total reaching 8.1 million.
地点与对象
暴发 发生在南达科他州,特别是在比德尔县(74,900 只商业火鸡群)和布鲁尔县(2,800 只后院火鸡群)。受影响的种群包括商业鸟类和后院鸟类。
事件
禽流感,特别是 高致病性禽流感 病毒 (H5N1),已被证实影响了南达科他州的两只鸡群。这种疾病传播迅速,并迅速杀死鸟类,促使对整个受影响鸡群实施安乐死的标准做法。
规模、影响与风险
The outbreak has affected 77,700 birds in South Dakota, contributing to a 4-year total of 8.1 million cases. The highly pathogenic nature of the virus poses a significant risk to bird populations and may have implications for public health and animal welfare.
应对、状态与不确定性
联邦官员确认了 暴发,并且对整个鸡群实施安乐死的标准做法已经到位。该文章没有提及正在进行的调查或超出此标准回应的其他措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Avian flu affects 77,700 SD birds, raising state’s 4-year outbreak total to 8.1 million

News Brief
Avian flu has returned to South Dakota, with federal officials confirming detections in two flocks this month. The highly pathogenic avian influenza virus, specifically H5N1, struck a commercial turkey flock of 74,900 in Beadle County on August 12 and a backyard flock of 2,800 birds in Brule County on Thursday. The standard response is to euthanize the entire affected flock, which is a common practice when the disease is confirmed. This outbreak adds to a 4-year total of 8.1 million cases in the state, highlighting the rapid spread and high mortality rate of the virus. The article does not mention any additional measures or ongoing investigations beyond the standard response, indicating that the situation is being managed through routine procedures. The outbreak raises concerns about the impact on bird populations and potential public health risks, though no human infections are reported in the supplied evidence.

The highly pathogenic avian influenza virus — H5N1, a strain that spreads quickly and kills birds rapidly — struck a flock of 74,900 commercial meat turkeys in Beadle County on Aug. 12 and a backyard flock of 2,800 birds in Brule County on Thursday. View five news elements
Time
The article was published on August 26, 2026. The avian flu outbreak in South Dakota was reported to have affected 77,700 birds in the state, with the 4-year outbreak total reaching 8.1 million.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in South Dakota, specifically in Beadle County (commercial turkey flock of 74,900) and Brule County (backyard flock of 2,800 birds). The affected population includes both commercial and backyard birds.
Event
Avian flu, specifically the highly pathogenic avian influenza virus (H5N1), was confirmed to have affected two flocks in South Dakota. The disease spreads quickly and kills birds rapidly, prompting the standard practice of euthanizing the entire affected flock.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has affected 77,700 birds in South Dakota, contributing to a 4-year total of 8.1 million cases. The highly pathogenic nature of the virus poses a significant risk to bird populations and may have implications for public health and animal welfare.
Response, status and uncertainty
Federal officials confirmed the outbreak, and the standard practice of euthanizing entire flocks is in place. The article does not mention ongoing investigations or additional measures beyond this standard response.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

长鼻海狗验出H5N1 澳洲首哺乳动物染禽流感 - 亚洲电视新闻

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报南澳大利亚海滩港地区一只长鼻海狗检测呈阳性后,澳大利亚卫生当局确认该国首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感。这只动物被发现死亡,没有明显的伤害,当局表示,这起案件似乎与其他海洋哺乳动物同时死亡无关。自 2026 年 6 月以来,澳大利亚一直在 野生鸟类 中与不断扩大的 H5N1 暴发 作斗争,该病毒现已蔓延到海洋哺乳动物物种中。截至 8 月 21 日,国家当局已记录了 297 例 H5 确诊病例,但尚未在家禽或农业环境中发现检测报告。联邦官员警告称,一旦H5 禽流感在环境中广泛传播,重大损失就难以避免。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-23;活动日期2026年8月23日。
地点与对象
澳大利亚南澳大利亚;海滨小镇海滩港;野生长鼻毛皮海豹种群(约 100,000 只动物)。
事件
澳大利亚当局证实该国首例哺乳动物感染 H5N1 禽流感,死者是在南澳大利亚海滩港发现的一只长鼻海狗。该事件已通过实验室测试得到证实。
规模、影响与风险
一例确诊病例出现在野生长鼻海豹;未报告人类感染或家禽养殖场检测阳性。该病毒在野生鸟类中高度传染,自2026年6月起在澳大利亚引发大规模海鸟死亡。联邦当局截至2026-08-21报告全国共297例确诊H5 禽流感病例。进一步在野生动物中传播的风险已被明确指出。
应对、状态与不确定性
联邦农业部长柯林斯表示,海豹的死亡似乎与其他患病或死亡的海洋哺乳动物无关。当局正在监控事态发展;没有证据表明 跨物种溢出 对家禽或农业有影响。起源和 传播 途径仍在研究中。证据中没有描述针对人类或家畜的具体公共卫生措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
长鼻海狗验出H5N1 澳洲首哺乳动物染禽流感 - 亚洲电视新闻
News Brief
Australian health authorities confirmed the country’s first mammal infection with H5N1 avian influenza after a long-nosed fur seal tested positive in South Australia’s Beachport region. The animal was found dead with no visible injuries, and authorities said the case appears unrelated to concurrent die-offs among other marine mammals. Since June 2026, Australia has been battling a widening H5N1 outbreak in wild birds that has now spilled over into a marine mammal species. As of August 21, national authorities had recorded 297 confirmed H5 cases, though no detections have been reported in poultry or agricultural settings. Federal officials warned that once H5 avian influenza circulates widely in the environment, substantial losses become difficult to avoid.

View five news elements
Time
Published 2026-08-23; event date 2026-08-23.
Location and population
South Australia, Australia; coastal town of Beachport; wild long-nosed fur seal population (~100,000 animals).
Event
Australian authorities confirmed the country's first mammal infection with H5N1 avian influenza in a long-nosed fur seal found dead in Beachport, South Australia. The event is confirmed by laboratory testing.
Scale, impact and risk
One confirmed case in a wild long-nosed fur seal; no reported human infections or poultry farm detections. The virus is highly contagious in wild birds and has caused large-scale seabird die-offs in Australia since June 2026. Federal authorities reported 297 confirmed H5 avian influenza cases nationwide as of 2026-08-21. Risk of further spread in wildlife is explicitly stated.
Response, status and uncertainty
Federal Agriculture Minister Collins stated the seal's death appeared unrelated to other sick or dead marine mammals. Authorities are monitoring the situation; no evidence of spillover to poultry or agriculture has been reported. The origin and transmission pathway remain under investigation. No specific public-health measures targeting humans or domestic animals are described in the evidence.
